# The Jewel of Electional Astrology
<note>Original title: Muhurtaratnam. "Muhurta" refers to auspicious time-selection or electional astrology.</note>

End:

->From the midst of the great ocean of milk that is Astrology,<-
->tossed by the great fish of the Sun, Moon, and other planets,<-
->this Jewel of Auspicious Timing was extracted by Govinda<-
->for the benefit of the world.<-
<note>Original: "Muhurtamani" (Jewel of Timing). The verse uses a metaphor of the Churning of the Ocean of Milk from Hindu mythology.</note>

->The New Moon falling on a Wednesday, the seventh lunar day on a Sunday,<-
->and the fourth lunar day on a Tuesday—these three days are considered inexhaustible.<-
<note>Inexhaustible (Akshaya) implies that spiritual merits earned on these specific alignments never diminish.</note>

->Salutations to Lord Krishna. May there be prosperity.<-

**Colophon:** Thus ends the *Jewel of Electional Astrology*.

**Author —** Govindapujyapada

**Commentary:** Parameshwara.

**Remarks:** The last portion of the text discloses that the work is also known as the "Jewel of Timing" <note>Original: Muhurthamani</note>. The commentator was the grandson of the author's disciples. <note>Original text spells "disciples" as "desciples".</note>

**Manuscript T<sub>1</sub>**      The Jewel of Electional Astrology (With Commentary)
<note>Original: "Savyakhyanam" means accompanied by an explanatory gloss or commentary.</note>

**No. 750 A.      C.O.L. No. 144 ( A and B )**

**Substance:** Paper. **Size:** 13 ½" x 8 ½". **Pages:** 379. 17 lines per page and 16 letters per line.

**Script:** Devanagari. **Number of Stanzas:** 3250. <note>Original: "Granthas" refers to a unit of measurement for the length of a text, equivalent to 32 syllables.</note> **Complete.**

**Owner:** C.O. **Subject:** Astrology.

Beginning:

->I take refuge in that Light of perfect consciousness and bliss,<-
->the giver of the Moon as an ornament, whose play of independence is Time,<-
->and of whom the multitude of stars is but a tiny spark.<-

->Though the teachings on auspicious timing were hidden by great masters<-
->such as Suvarnachuta, who kissed the very ocean of the science of light,<-
->they are here revealed in a mere direction.<-

# Introduction

The four junctions <note>The "sandhyas" or transitional periods of the day</note> of the Sun and Moon according to the Lāṭa and Vaidhṛta systems; portents such as earthquakes, thunderbolts, tremors, comets, meteors, and the invisibility of the moon.

Now he concludes—
From the midst of the great ocean of milk, where the lights of the Sun and Moon flicker like great fish, this "Jewel of Electional Astrology" <note>original: "Muhurtaratna"</note> was extracted by Govinda for the benefit of the world.

Thus ends the fourth chapter in the commentary on the *Jewel of Electional Astrology*, composed by Parameshwara, the grandson of Govinda's disciple.

## Description of the Symbols Used in This Book

For the sake of convenience, certain symbols have been used by the researcher in this work. The details of these symbols are specified below.

T = Manuscript <term>Matrika; a handwritten source text</term> from the Oriental Research Institute (O.R.I.), Trivandrum, No. 131.

T₁ = Manuscript from O.R.I. Trivandrum, No. 144. (A;B).

M = Manuscript from the Government Oriental Manuscripts Library (G.O.M.L.), Madras, No. 3295.

[ ] = 1. Where there is no basis in either the commentary or the original text, yet a correction has been made by the researcher according to the context, the corrected portion is shown within these square brackets.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;2. Portions not present in the original text but added in some places to preserve the poetic meter <note>original: "chhandas"</note> are shown in the aforementioned brackets.

( ) = 1. The letters 'pa' and 'va' are frequently seen interchanged in the manuscripts. In such instances, these rounded parentheses are used.

&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;2. Where a slight necessary change has been made to a specific letter according to the context, the corrected portion is also placed in these parentheses.

See Comm. <note>original: "dra. vya." for drashtavya-vyakhyanam</note> = This symbol is used where the original text has been corrected according to the commentary.

See Orig. <note>original: "dra. mu." for drashtavya-mulam</note> = This symbol is used in places where the commentary has been corrected according to the original text.

During the editing of this book, changes or suggestions have been made according to my best judgment where required. Somewhere, due to oversight or ignorance, an error might have been committed by me. The corrected portions are numbered, and the original readings existing in the manuscripts are shown exactly as they are in the Footnotes. The rules for the aforementioned symbols have been maintained from the beginning to the end of this research treatise. If there is a slip anywhere, this person <note>the editor/author</note> should be forgiven by the kind-hearted readers who favor only good qualities.

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments

> I take refuge in that Light of perfect consciousness and bliss, the source of the Moon, whose tiny spark of independent creative power constitutes the entire host of heavenly bodies. || 1
> 
> Although the teachings on auspicious moments <term>Muhūrta: the science of choosing the right time for actions</term> have been kept hidden by great scholars like Suvarṇacūta and others who have kissed the ocean of astrology <gloss>1</gloss>, they are shown here in brief outline. || 2

---

<gloss>2</gloss> Salutations to the holy Lord Gaṇeśa. May there be no obstacles.
> I bow to the Lord who is half-woman <note>original: "Ardhanārīśvara," the union of Shiva and Parvati</note>, the treasure-house of knowledge and abode of mercy, who shines like reddish gold and is adorned by the glittering crescent moon.
> 
> By the venerable Govinda, whose heart was sprinkled with the water of compassion, this "Jewel of Auspicious Moments" was extracted from the milk-ocean of the astrological scriptures.
> 
> A small portion <gloss>4</gloss> of that work is now being explained by his student’s grandson, Īśvara <gloss>3</gloss>, according to what has been heard from the ancestors.

**Regarding the phrase "Whose independence...":** The "tiny sparks" refer to the fragments of divine knowledge. The word "whose" is implied here. <gloss>A</gloss> <note>The commentator suggests the original might have intended "ada" instead of "yat".</note>
Because it has been perceived through one's own senses, I say "I take refuge" or "I serve." Here, by the power of praising the nature of Time and the Planets, it is shown that the science of astrology—which depends on Time and the Planets—is being addressed.
<gloss>5</gloss> **Regarding "Suvarṇacūta...":** In this upcoming treatise, the "teachings on auspicious moments" refers to the recitation of the traditional astrological texts. In short, the essence is being shown. That which was <gloss>6</gloss> concealed by Suvarṇacūta and others, the teaching known as "The Accomplishment of All Aims" <note>original: "Sarvasiddhi"</note>, is also being shown here in brief <gloss>7</gloss>. This Suvarṇacūta is said to be a noble twice-born <term>Brahmin</term> from the author's own village. Previously, a certain chief among Brahmins composed the Sarvasiddhi-Muhūrta scripture. It was not accepted by some; therefore, for the benefit of that noble Brahmin—

# Now, the Flaws of the Auspicious Moment (Muhurta)

The astrological flaws include: the *Lata* <note>a specific planetary obstruction</note>, *Vaidhrita* <note>an inauspicious yoga</note>, solar and lunar eclipses, the four twilights, thunderclaps, lightning strikes, earthquakes, <margin>1. Reading from Manuscript M. Manuscript T reads: "Comets and meteors"</margin> the appearance of comets and meteors, and the invisibility of the moon <note>the New Moon phase</note>. || 3 ||
<margin>2. The moon being lost/invisible.</margin>

The howling of jackals and other animals, <margin>3. T reads: "within the mind." M reads: "darkness/wealth."</margin> internal distress, the sight of unpleasant things, the *Vishwa-Pradosha* <note>a specific evening period on the 13th lunar day</note>, the night itself, and proximity to fire or water. || 4 ||

The appearance of a rainbow at night, the *Gandanta* <note>unlucky junctions between zodiac signs</note>, the astrologer <term>Daivajna</term> <note>literally "knower of fate"</note> <margin>4. M is partially illegible here.</margin> having a shaved head, or the astrologer being interrupted while sleeping or eating, or having disheveled hair or suffering from disease. || 5 ||

Impurity, exhaustion, <margin>5. M reads: "Shiva-ushna" (Heat of Shiva).</margin> the "Hot-Headed" period, the "Poisonous Minutes" <term>Visha-Nadika</term>, the <margin>6. M reading. T reads: "Yogemandā".</margin> Sun's entry into a new sign <term>Sankranti</term>, and the "Burned" <term>Dagda</term> combinations, as well as the days of Saturn and Mars. || 6 ||

The day of Jupiter after midday, one's own "Danger Day" <note>the 3rd, 5th, or 7th birth star</note>, <margin>7. T1 reads: "Perception." M reads: "Pratyaksharabham."</margin> the "Execution" or "Enemy" stars, the Moon in the eighth house <margin>8. T1 reads: "Moon-se."</margin> <term>Randhra-Chandra</term>, and the <margin>9. T1 reads: "Danashau."</margin> 88th and 108th degrees from the birth moon. || 7 ||

---

->It is heard that the teacher Govindacharya made great efforts to master all these accomplishments. By saying "The statement of the auspicious moment is seen," it is indicated that the text is written exactly as it appears in the traditional scriptures <term>Agamas</term> composed by ancient teachers, without changing the words. The meaning and specific details are written elsewhere in the form of a commentary.<-

Now, he explains the flaws—**Lata**, etc. From the mention of "Sun and Moon planets," we understand solar and lunar eclipses. *Nirghata* refers to a great wind accompanied by a loud sound. *Ketu* refers to the sight of a comet. "Jackals and others" refers to jackals, buffaloes, and so on. "Internal" refers to the mind. *Vishwa-Pradosha* is the twilight at sunset on the thirteenth lunar day. "Shaving the head" refers to the act of tonsure. "Sleeping, etc." refers to the state of the astrologer. "Hot-headed" refers to the *Ushna-shikha* period. *Sankranti* is the Sun's transition. By the word "Burned, etc.," he refers to the "Fire-mouth" and "Great Destroyer" inauspicious combinations. "One's own" refers to the person performing the ritual. "Moon in the eighth" refers to the eighth lunar sign or house. <margin>10. T reads: "Labheti."</margin> *Harda* refers to the eighty-eighth degree. <margin>11. T reads: "Danavo" (the demon) is 108.</margin> *Danapo* refers to one hundred and eight. This means the 88th and 108th degrees from the position of the moon at birth.

# Chapter One

When the lunar portions, the periods of Vishti and Gulika <note>Vishti is an inauspicious timing (Karana); Gulika is a "son of Saturn" representing a malefic point in the sky</note>, and the aspects of Rahu and Ketu <note>The North and South Nodes of the Moon</note> occur; and when the rising of these is likewise not favorable; || 8 ||

Even if the specific moment (Muhurta) or the planetary transit (Chara) is considered, any auspicious action performed then is far from successful. The faults known as the "Vedas" (4), "Arrows" (5), "Sages" (7), and "Openings" (9) <gloss>1</gloss> <note>original: "Sarpamurdhaikargalah" - referring to the "Serpent's Head" and the "Single Bolt" obstacles</note>—these are the Single Bolt obstacles. || 9 ||

The void of the lunar day and asterism, the "Blind Star," the "Day-Destroyer," and "Death"; <gloss>2</gloss> the "Thorn-Pillar," the "Blow of the Half-Watch," and the "Staff of Yama" <gloss>3</gloss> <note>Yama-kantaka is a specific malefic time-segment governed by the Lord of Death</note>; || 10 ||

The asterism occupied by a planet, the Six Faults, <gloss>4</gloss> the combustion of Jupiter and others, the "Omitted Day" (Avama), the "Triple-Touch" Day <gloss>5</gloss> <gloss>6</gloss>, the "Empty" lunar days (Rikta), and the New Moon and Eighth lunar days; || 11 ||

The stars Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, and Mula; the three "Prior" stars, Yamya, and Pitrya; the "Sieve" star, <gloss>7</gloss> and the divisions of the malefic planets—and their placement in the Cardines (1st, 4th, 7th, 10th houses) or Trines (1st, 5th, 9th houses); || 12 ||

The state of Venus being combust, the Moon being in an enemy’s sign, all planets being in the 12th or 8th houses, as well as the triad of birth stars; || 13 ||

The "rising of these" refers to the rising of the malefics <gloss>8</gloss> such as Rahu and Ketu. Here, because Rahu and Ketu are mentioned first, it implies that later, the prohibition of malefics being situated in the Cardines or Trines applies.
Some <gloss>9</gloss> remember it thus: "That planetary transit in which Rahu and Ketu are primary is the transit accepted here."
And is that calculated by the motion of the Sun or by the portions of the signs? The phrase "even if the Muhurta" implies an inquiry into the specific auspicious moment.

"Auspicious action then" <gloss>11</gloss> means an auspicious action performed at that time. <gloss>12</gloss> It means it is "extremely inauspicious." The terms "Vedas-Arrows-Sages-Openings" <gloss>13</gloss> refer to the numbers four, five, seven, and nine. Those are the faults. "Single Bolt" (Ekargala) is a specific obstacle. "Blow of the Half-Watch" refers to a specific division of the day. A lunar day (Tithi) that is not visible at two consecutive sunrises...

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments

->**The Striking of the Feet, the Time of Smells, and Planetary Positions**<-

The striking of the feet <note>original: "padāghāta"; a specific astrological flaw</note>, the time of smells <note>original: "gandhakāla"</note>, and the position of planets in the house of death <note>original: "mṛtibhāva"; the 8th house in a horoscope</note> are condemned in all auspicious activities, but not in the general deliberation of timing. || 14 ||

The flaws known as *Lata* <note>planetary "kick"</note>, *Vaidhrita*, the conjunctions of the Sun and Moon, the twilights, the "fiery-headed" period <note>original: "shikhoshnika"</note>, and the *Vishva* pulses <note>original: "nadi"</note>—these are not altered by any exceptions. || 15 ||

When the Moon is "lost" <note>original: "nashtendu"; the New Moon or waning moon</note>, if it is placed in a favorable division <note>varga</note> and aspected by benefic planets, it does not cause the fault of suffering. The increase or decrease of a lunar day <note>tithi</note> is considered equal in both the bright and dark fortnights. || 16 ||

On the thirteenth lunar day, the twilight is measured; it is beneficial for the mind. One should observe silence for half the night combined with one-eighth of the day. || 17 ||

At the junction of the constellations Magha and Ashlesha, two pulses each; at the junction of Jyeshtha and Mula, two each; and at the junction of Revati and Ashvini, three pulses each are known as the "ocean-numbered" <note>four</note> pulses. || 18 ||

The "End of the Knot" <note>term: <term>Gandanta</term> - a transitional period between zodiac signs and lunar mansions considered spiritually volatile</note> occurs at the final portions of Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces, as well as the initial portions of Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius. || 19 ||

---

On that lunar day, it is called an "omitted day" <note>original: "avamadina"</note>. That lunar day which is seen at two consecutive sunrises is called "triple-touching" <note>original: "trispṛg"</note>.

"The striking of the feet" refers to what will be described as striking with the feet and so on. "Planetary position in the house of death" refers to whichever planet is in a position that grants death. The aforementioned flaws, such as those numbered by the Vedas <note>four</note>, are condemned in auspicious actions, but not in the general consideration of a *Muhurta* <note>term: <term>Muhurta</term> - a traditional unit of time (48 minutes) or the selection of an auspicious moment</note>. Now, the characteristics and exceptions of the stated flaws are shown. Regarding the lack of exceptions for *Lata* and others, it says: "Lata..." etc. "They are not altered by exceptions" means that no exception exists. By the word "etcetera," it is inferred that the rising times of *Vishti* <note>an inauspicious astrological period</note> and others are included. This is because of the statement: "Even if occurring at that time, those outside the specific hours of their own rising are not to be taken."

"In a favorable division" is the exception for the "lost Moon." An increase in the lunar day beyond sixty pulses <note>nadikas</note> is called "Tithi-increase."

# Chapter One

> This number should be known respectively at the end of the fifth lunar day, the tenth lunar day, and the end of the full/new moon phase; it is also seen in the divisions of Guha <note>The number 6, referring to the deity Kartikeya</note>, the Lord of Wealth <note>The number 11, referring to Kubera</note>, and Fire <note>The number 1, referring to Agni</note>, and so on. || 20 ||

> One should subtract the "fiery crest" <note>original: "Ushna Shikha," a specific inauspicious period</note> ghatis <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> in the portions of Mars, Saturn, and the Sun, and in the "oceans" <note>the number 4</note> respectively. One should know the rows of half-portions, three, eight, nine, and ten; in all cases, the final one excludes eight. || 21 ||

> At the end of Capricorn <note>Mṛga</note> and the beginning of Aquarius <note>Kalaśa</note>, it proceeds through contact. Some wise men have said that this row exists in the "ocean" <note>4</note> constellations. || 22 ||

> By the limb itself, the sky, water, air, and the actor; the passionate one, the naked one, the one with claws; the divine, the man of the nation, through fear; by the enemies and actors, by the instruments, the man is the praiser of heroes. <note>This verse uses a mnemonic system where specific syllables represent numerical values for time calculations.</note>

Decrease means loss. The "final portions" are the final Navamshas <note>the 1/9th division of a zodiac sign</note>. "Arrows" <note>Shara</note> means the number five. Those are called the "junction points" <note>Gandanta</note>. Guha is the sixth lunar day. The Lord of Wealth is the eleventh. Fire is the first lunar day.

By the phrase "Mars and Saturn," the "fiery crest" <note>Ushna Shikha</note> is indicated. The portion of Mars is the portion of Aries. The portion of Saturn <note>Ara</note> is the portion of Scorpio. The portion of the Sun is the portion of Capricorn. <insert>In the five portions including Aries</insert> in the constellations of Ashvini, Rohini, Punarvasu, etc. At the end of the Aries portion, there are seven and a half nadikas <note>another term for ghati, 24 minutes</note> known as the "fiery crest." Likewise, in the four "oceans" <note>4 constellations</note> including the Aries portion: in the constellations starting with Vishakha <note>Shurpa</note>, there are eight nadis at the beginning of the Aries portion. Likewise, in the five starting with Krittika including the Capricorn portion, there are nine nadis at the end of the Capricorn portion. In the four starting with Jyeshtha, there are ten nadis at the end of the Capricorn portion. Regarding "the final one excludes eight": in all these portions, one should know the measure of the nadikas <margin>The final measure is slow-moving</margin>. However, the measure of eight nadis is to be known at the beginning of the Navamsha. The row-number mentioned in the four constellations starting with Jyeshtha proceeds through contact at the end of Capricorn and the beginning of Aquarius. Some wise men say they exist together. The meaning is: there are five nadis at the end of the Capricorn portion, and five nadis at the beginning of the Aquarius portion. Following the nadis shown by the two syllables "Limb" <note>Anga</note> etc., there are four nadis.

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology

The completion [of a period] and the daily rise [of a planet]; the poet <note>The planet Venus</note>, the night, the orb, the night<sup>1</sup> or the sun-remover; in the directions starting with the Fire-god <note>The Krittika constellation, associated with Agni/Fire</note> and others, having passed those, the four ghatikas <note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> thereafter are considered "poisonous" (Visha). || 23 ||

In the constellation of Shiva <note>Ardra</note>, some say "the best" (vara)<sup>3</sup>, while others read it as "beyond" (para)<sup>4</sup>. According to the established tradition of the elders, both readings are considered correct. || 24 ||

### Regarding the Entry of the Sun (Sankranti)

<sup>6</sup> <insert>A</insert> Multiply the Kali era year by the number "Jagannupura" <note>A mnemonic code for a specific large number used in astronomical calculations</note>; the seventh part of that will be the day. From the remainder, the entry (Sankranti)<sup>7</sup> occurs, calculated from nine ghatikas<sup>8</sup> arising over two days. || 25 ||

[The calculation] should stand following the Kali year, which is the "nourisher of the earth." From this, the Equinox entry (Vishuvat-Sankranti) is also desired for the city and the night. || 26 ||

---

In the directions of the Fire-god (Havyasha) and others, meaning starting from the Krittika constellation, the periods known as "poisonous" (Visha) occur. The letters at the end of the verses are for completing the meter. Regarding the phrase "both are correct":

The time of the Equinox entry is described by the phrase "That Fortune, the anklet of the world" (Sa Shri Jagannupura) and so on. The result of the division by this [formula] gives the Kali-day <term>Kali-dina: the number of days elapsed since the start of the current cosmic age</term>. From the remainder multiplied by sixty, the ghatikas and other units of the entry<sup>9</sup> are found. From this result of days and ghatikas, when two days and nine ghatikas are subtracted, and the days are again divided by seven, the time of the entry starting from Friday is determined. In this way, the time ending in ghatikas is to be calculated. When the entry time ending in vighatikas <note>24 seconds</note> is to be calculated, one should subtract two days, nine ghatikas, and fifteen vighatikas. In this entry time, one should apply the correction for the "ascensional difference" (Caradala) and the "difference in longitude" (Deshantara) by the inversion of the planets. That time<sup>10</sup> becomes accurate (sphuta) for one's own location. If the Equinox entry time is calculated for the purpose of a constant (dhruva), then only the longitude correction (Deshantara) should be applied. The ascensional difference (Caradala) mentioned as "desired for the city and night" is brought forth by the phrase "arising<sup>11</sup> from nine ghatikas and two days"; therefore, it is a gross or approximate calculation. In that, again, fifteen vighatikas...

# Chapter One

<page-num>7</page-num>
<header>Chapter One</header>

->The one who knows various forms, the fierce and heroic Lord <note>original: "Bhagavan"</note>, has appeared for us in the arena. Like Rudra <note>the roarer/destroyer</note> appearing through a bamboo thicket, he is the lover of the cowherdesses <note>Gopis</note> and travels in a divine celestial vehicle. (27)<-

->My soul is eternal, without a known lineage, like the sun <note>original: "Savita"</note> but not the mere orb <note>original: "Bhanu"</note>; it is an effort toward the water-jar <note>a metaphor for the vessel of time or the head</note>. The minutes and other units marking the end of the day are the phrases of the Sun upon its entry. (28)<-

One should read this after purifying the "desired night of the body" <note>a technical term for a specific time calculation</note>. Here, the reading "Bhairava" is made in reference to the day of the clear equinox. From the sentence starting with Venus and others, if two days are again subtracted, the sentence for the solar day <note>original: "Arkavara"</note> is produced. This solar day calculation is called "the desired night of the city." In the sentences regarding the Sun's transit <note>original: "Sankranti"</note> beginning with "Mamarir," certain minutes should be added to the final remainders. The Teacher <note>Acharya</note> has merely shown a sentence that was not recited by any previous author of such verses. Those who know subtle mathematics <note>original: "Sukshma-ganita"</note> read it as follows:

> “The peaks of Meru are perfumed in heaven, and are heard of even by Indra. One bows to Rudra, who is distinguished by his lineage of fear, the supreme devotee traveling in the celestial path. With a body of soft earth, devoid of water, undecayed in prosperity, this man is of noble lineage. <insert>Undecayed in prosperity, this man is of noble lineage.</insert> The minutes and other units marking the end of the day are the phrases of the Sun upon its entry.”

The word "Transit" <note>original: "Sankranti"</note> is used to indicate the southern solstice and others; thus it is said:

> “The months beginning with Capricorn <note>original: "Mriga"</note> constitute the southern and northern solstices <note>original: "Ayana"</note>. These are conceived only through the Sun, not through the tropical zodiac <note>original: "Sayana"</note>. In the science of observing the southern path, the degrees of the planets' solstices are taken; however, in the movement through the signs, these are not required. The zodiacal signs are formed by the pairs of Nakshatras beginning with Ashvini plus one quarter. The signs beginning with Aries <note>original: "Aja"</note> are produced from them, and their position is then fixed in the lunar mansions. The conjunction of the Moon is seen only when the degrees are equal. The conjunction of stars is thus manifest, but the true positions are distinct. When the natural solstices beginning with Capricorn and Cancer are explained by the wise, they are then called 'altered' <note>original: "Vaikrita"</note> by the term 'Para'.”

And so says Varahamihira <note>a famous 6th-century Indian astronomer</note>:

> “If the Sun turns back without reaching Capricorn, it destroys the South and the West. If it turns back without reaching Cancer, it destroys the North and the East.” Having passed the northern solstice, it turns back...

<page-num>8</page-num>
-># The Gem of Electional Astrology<-
<note>original: "Muhurtaratnam"</note>

During the Solstices, the Equinoxes, and other entries of the Sun into zodiac signs <note>Sankranti</note>, one must avoid three days, one day, and thirty *nadis* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> respectively on both sides of the transition. || 29 ||

The evening of the Southern Solstice <note>Dakshinayana</note> and the dark fortnight of the lunar month are considered censurable for auspicious acts. Starting from Capricorn, the Sun’s passage through pairs of signs creates the six seasons: Dewy, Spring, Summer, Rainy, Autumn, and the sixth is called Winter. || 30 ||

The three seasons beginning with the Dewy season <note>Shishira</note> constitute the Northern Path <note>Uttarayana</note>, which is called the Day of the Gods. The other three seasons constitute the Southern Path, which is declared to be the Night of the Gods. || 31 ||

### Now, the Afflictions of Weekdays, Lunar Days, and Constellations

If the constellations Vishakha, Shatabhishak, Ardra, or Uttarashadha coincide with a Tuesday and fall on a "Void" lunar day <note>Rikta Tithi: the 4th, 9th, or 14th days of a lunar fortnight</note>, these combinations bring death to those performing the work. || 32 ||

<insert>The following are "Burnt Combinations" <note>Dagda Yogas</note>, which are highly inauspicious:</insert>
Wednesday with the 2nd lunar day; Tuesday with the 5th; Thursday with the 6th; Friday with the 8th; Saturday or Monday with the 11th; Saturday with the 9th; and Sunday with the 12th. || 33 ||

---

This brings safety and the growth of crops. Even if the Sun stays in its natural state, any unnatural movement <note>retrograde or erratic motion</note> creates fear. Thus, the Elder Garga says:
> "If the Sun is bright and clear during the Solstice, one should predict good rain, plenty, and general well-being. Whether the Sun has not yet turned, is at the midpoint, or has completed its turn, it is praised; but if it is diminished in luster, it brings fear, famine, and pestilence to the world."
Therefore, in matters of *Muhurta* <note>choosing an auspicious moment</note>, the Solstices and transitions must be understood through the Sun alone.

The verses starting with "Vishakha" describe the "Burnt Combinations." Regarding the verse "Wednesday and the 2nd": this verse is found in the *Barhaspatya* <note>an ancient text attributed to the sage Brihaspati</note>. Therefore, we are merely explaining its application. "Wednesday with the 2nd" implies the lunar day. "On the day of Jupiter" <note>Thursday</note> is understood. "Friday and the 8th" is one compound term. The grammatical case is either the first or the seventh. "The 11th with the Moon <note>Monday</note> or Saturn..."

# Chapter One

The months <note>represented by the number 12</note>, the Rudras <note>11</note>, the directions <note>10</note>, Rama <note>3</note>, the six <note>6</note>, and the twin physicians <note>2</note>, and the sages <note>7</note> in order; these lunar days <term>Tithis: the lunar day in Vedic timekeeping</term> starting from Sunday are known as the "Combust Unions" <term>Dagda Yogas: literally 'burnt' or 'scorched' combinations deemed unlucky</term>. || 34 ||

The lunar days starting from the sixth, when joined with the days of the week starting from Monday, are known as the seven "Fire-Consuming Unions" <term>Hutashana Yogas</term> in both fortnights of the month. || 35 ||

The Sun’s day <note>Sunday</note> falling on the Magha star; the Moon’s day <note>Monday</note> on Vishakha; Mars’s day <note>Tuesday</note> on Ardra; and Wednesday on Mula; || 36 ||

Thursday on Shatabhisha; Friday on Rohini; and Saturday on Ashadha; these combinations are the "Great Destroyers." || 37 ||

Ashvini, Chitra, Uttarashadha, Dhanishtha, Aryaman <note>Uttara Phalguni</note>, Jyeshtha, and Revati; these stars joined with the days starting from Sunday create the "Thunderbolt Union" <term>Ashani Yoga</term>, which destroys all works. || 38 ||

Bharani, Anuradha, Punarvasu, Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, Magha, and Shravana; these in order from Sunday are the unions called "Thunderbolt" and "Poison." || 39 ||

Those unions known as "Combust" or "Death-like" do not cause harm if Jupiter <note>the planet of wisdom and protection</note> or the Lord of the Ascendant is aspecting or occupying the Rising Sign <term>Lagna: the specific zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>. || 40 ||

---

...is a single word. There, the relationship between the first-mentioned eleventh lunar day and the Moon would follow the established order. Thus, Saturday remains. By the remainder, it falls on the ninth day. Or, "Eleventh" is one word, and "Moon-Saturday" is another. Here too, the relationship follows the previous order. The arrangement is that when the specified weekday and lunar day coincide, it becomes a "Combust Union." Therefore, the seventh day is also applicable. "Months" refers to the twelfth day. "Rudras" refers to the eleventh. As an exception to the rule regarding the days of Saturn and Mars, the general faults of the weekdays are addressed in the section beginning with "Faults of the Day."

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology

The lord of the day <note>the planet ruling the specific day of the week</note>, if positioned in the third, eleventh, sixth, or second house from the natal sign, destroys the flaws associated with that day. Even a cruel <note>malefic</note> planet, if retrograde or defeated in planetary combat, is considered to lose its cruelty. || 41 ||

The Moon, when placed in a favorable division <note>Varga</note>, performing auspicious actions, and aspected by benefic planets, removes the flaws of the three birth stars, the "counter-agent" star, the "danger" star, and the "death" star <note>referring to the Janma, Pratyari, Vipad, and Vadha positions in the Navatara system</note>. || 42 ||

Each factor, such as being in a favorable division, acts as an individual exception <note>neutralizer</note> to the flaws. If the lord of the eighth house from the birth sign is a friend to the birth lord, then even the eighth sign <gloss>1</gloss> is considered excellent. || 43 ||

If the lord of the "action" house <note>10th house</note> is a friend to the birth lord, the "destructive" degree is rendered harmless. By the word "Lord of the Eighth," the <gloss>2</gloss> Khara-pa <note>the ruler of the 22nd Drekkana or decan, usually considered highly inauspicious</note> is intended here. || 44 ||

Similarly, by the word "Lord of Action," the <gloss>3</gloss> ruler of the 88th division <note>Amshaka</note> is meant. In the bright fortnight, the sequence of the Lion and other Karana <note>half-tithis</note> begins from the second half of the <gloss>4</gloss> first lunar day. || 45 ||

### On the Subject of the Karanas

The Lion, the Tiger, the Boar, the Donkey, the Elephant, the Bull, and the Dog—these are the <gloss>5</gloss> mobile Karanas that occur in rotation. || 46 ||

The Bird, the Four-legged, the Serpent, and Kimstughna are the <gloss>6</gloss> fixed Karanas located at the end <note>of the lunar month</note>. <gloss>A</gloss> In the bright fortnight, the calculation is additive, and in the dark fortnight, it is subtractive. || 47 ||

One should know the "Plough" time; the <gloss>7</gloss> Ghatikas <note>units of 24 minutes</note> begin from the start of the day. Within the fourth lunar day, the end of the "Deer" Ghatikas marks the <gloss>8</gloss> subtle Vishti <note>an inauspicious timing</note> of Varuna. || 48 ||

---

"Defeated" refers to a planet that has not achieved victory in planetary combat <note>Graha Yuddha</note>. Regarding the verse "placed in a favorable division," each of these conditions serves as an exception to the flaws of the three birth stars. The "Khara-pa" is the lord of the twenty-second decan <note>Drekkana</note>. The "Lord of the 88th division" is the ruler of the eighty-eighth Navamsha. "Anala" refers to the first lunar day (Pratipat). The "Dog" refers to the Vishti Karana, and <gloss>9</gloss> "Kimstughna" refers to the Worm. The "fixed" Karanas are those located in the <gloss>10</gloss> four half-lunar days starting from the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight. <gloss>11</gloss> By the phrase "additive and subtractive," the subtle Vishti timing is determined...

# Chapter One

<sup>1</sup> On the eighth day, the <term>Vishti</term><note>Vishti is an inauspicious astrological period, often personified as a goddess or a shadow, during which certain actions are avoided and others are performed for protection.</note> arises in its designated direction; it remains within the directional <term>nadika</term><note>A unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note> of the poet's direction. By this method, one should deduce all these matters in other cases as well. || 49 ||

Time is to be led; salutations to the serpent-like one; the sky is a woman, but not that again. The "Subtle Vishti" has been described by others as being like a drop of water and sesame seeds on the head. || 50 ||

The Banyan, the Flame-of-the-Forest, the Bamboo, the Strychnine tree, and the Governor’s Plum; the Fire-flame bush and the Snuhi-karni—these are established as the **Trees of Vishti**. || 51 ||

During the "Blind Alignment," if these **Trees of Vishti** are placed in their own directions at that specific time and are properly consecrated, they provide protection from kings, thieves, and wild animals. || 52 ||

These trees were created by the Lord of the City <note>referring to Shiva</note> while he was destroying the sacrifice of Daksha. They are born from a portion of **Virabhadra** <note>A fierce avatar of Shiva</note> and are traditionally conceived of as "Dogs." || 53 ||

---

He explains the watch <note>Yama, a 3-hour period</note>, the minute-unit <note>Nadika</note>, and the direction for each lunar day. By the syllable *Bha*, the fourth lunar day is indicated; by the syllable *U*, the fifth direction (West) is indicated, as well as the fifth watch of the lunar day. In that watch, it is the fifth <term>nadika</term>. This means the Vishti rises at the end of the fifth <term>nadika</term>. In this way, the "Plough-Lunar-Day" should be understood. The direction, watch, and <term>nadika</term> are to be known through the vowels and consonants. <insert>The phrase "Shushi is firm" refers to the dark fortnight.</insert> <margin>The source for this phrase is not found; there may be a gap in the original manuscript.</margin> The explanation of this is on the fourth day. Regarding the phrase "at the end of the directional nadika": on the eighth day, Vishti occurs at the end of the tenth <term>nadika</term>, after one watch and two <term>nadikas</term> have passed.

He describes the <term>nadika</term> and direction of the "Subtle Vishti" according to another school of thought: "Time is to be led." Time here refers to the <sup>6</sup>thirty-first <term>nadika</term>. *Shi* indicates the Western direction. Thus, on the fourth day, it is "to be led." The tenth <term>nadika* is *Ra*, which is the Southeast direction. This applies to the eighth day.

The Banyan and others are the **Trees of Vishti**. The *Kinshuka* is the <gloss>Marikka</gloss> <note>The commentator uses a local vernacular name here, likely from a South Indian language.</note>. The *Vikankata* is the *Vaiyankata*. The *Ghataki* is the <gloss>Aruputi</gloss>; it is also read as *Ghataki Tatiri*. Then there is the *Snuhi-karni*. During the "Blind Alignment" (days without sight), at the time of the Vishti's rise, these trees are placed in their own directions. "Consecrated" means <sup>7</sup>meditating on them as having the form of Vishti (as will be described) and chanting the Vishti mantra. "Dogs" refers to female dogs or hounds.

# Now, regarding Gulika #

The eight portions of the day begin with the lord of the day;
The second half of Saturn's portion is called the rising of Gulika.
<note>Gulika is considered the "son" of Saturn and represents a specific sensitive point in the zodiac.</note>
The <term>instantaneous</term> <note>Sanskrit: tatkalika; refers to the precise moment of calculation</note> position is likewise found in its eighth part.
The portions of the night begin with the lord of the fifth day from the current day-lord. || 61 ||

The face of a woman, the forest, the swift—so much is to be given by Hari himself.
The beloved is eternal, the jewel is the measure; fie upon this, for it is a state of destruction. || 62 ||
<note>This verse uses a mnemonic system (likely Katapayadi notation) to encode numerical values for calculating Gulika's position on different days of the week.</note>

By multiplying the measures of day and night and dividing by thirty,
The very precise gain of <term>Mandi</term> <note>Another name for Gulika</note> is achieved; such is the rule of those who know the tradition. || 63 ||

***

In those six parts, the destruction of work and other ill effects occur in order. Such is the fruit here. By the phrase "extremely dark," the nature of <term>Vishti</term> <note>An inauspicious astrological period or "Karana"</note> is described. "Exerted" means diligent. He states the exception to Vishti with the phrase "from the lunar day."

He explains the time of Gulika with the verse "The eight..." The eight parts of the day begin with the <term>Lord of the Day</term> <note>The planet ruling the specific day of the week, e.g., the Sun for Sunday</note>; these seven planets rise in sequence. In the eighth part, the Lord of the Day rises again. Thus, there are eight day-portions accompanied by the risings of the lords starting with the day-lord. Among these, the second half of Saturn's portion <insert>Mandi's portion</insert> is designated as the rising of Gulika.

Furthermore, the eighth part of that second half of Saturn's portion becomes the instantaneous <term>Yama</term> <note>A division of time</note>. This is what is meant: the eight sub-portions of the second half of Saturn's portion also begin with the rising of the day-lord. Among those, the end of the second half of Saturn's portion is called the instantaneous rising of Gulika. Again, within those eight parts of Saturn's second half, the second half is called the rising of Gulika. In this way, the subtle Gulika is to be known. By repeating this process with eight parts of that second half, the achievement of the "very subtle" Gulika occurs. By doing this again, a Gulika even subtler than the subtle is to be known.

At night, however, the eight night-portions begin with the rising of the lord of the fifth day from the day-lord. Among those, the second half of Saturn's portion is called the rising of Gulika. The instantaneous position is to be known in the same way. He mentions Gulika according to another opinion with the phrase "The face of a woman..." On Sunday <note>Sanskrit: Arkavara</note>, the time of Gulika's rise is 20 <term>Nadikas</term> and 25 <term>Ghatikas</term> <note>Traditional units of time; 1 Nadika/Ghatika is 24 minutes</note>. These are the Gulika sentences <note>Mnemonic rules</note> for a day measured at thirty Nadikas. Elsewhere, their...

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments

The sine of the Sun’s longitude multiplied by the equinoctial shadow <note>original: "palāṅgula"; the length of a shadow cast by a gnomon at noon during the equinox</note> and divided by 180 <note>original: "vyomāṣṭa bhū"; literally 'space-eight-one' in the Katapayadi system</note> results in a value that is added or subtracted from thirty depending on whether the Sun is in the signs beginning with Aries or Libra; that determines the length of the day. || 64 ||
By the term "equinoctial fingers," the equinoctial shadow is intended. A strong Lord of the Day <note>the planet ruling the specific day of the week</note> situated in the Ascendant is auspicious and removes the blemish of Gulika <note>a malefic mathematical point in space, often considered the son of Saturn</note>. || 65 ||

### Now, the Blemishes

From the constellation occupied by the Sun, the 10th, 7th, 15th, and 21st constellations—
when associated with <insert>meteors</insert>, earthquakes, the "Staff of Brahma" <note>a rare atmospheric phenomenon or comet</note>, and "The Banner"—
occurring at night, always, or during the morning or evening,
for a duration of seven, ten, and six ghatikas <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>, these are extremely censurable. || 66 ||
An earthquake shakes one from their position; a meteor burns the lineage.
The Staff of Brahma leads to destruction; in the case of the Banner, death is foretold. || 67 ||

---

Regarding the refinement, he says: "Day and night..." One should multiply those sentences <note>mnemonic tables</note> by the measures of the day and night in ghatikas and divide by thirty. The results are the highly precise "Gulika-sentences." By the word "measure," the ghatikas are intended.

Regarding the calculation of the day's length, he says: "Equinoctial fingers..." <margin>T1: Equinoctial finger-one</margin> The equinoctial fingers are the inches of the equinoctial shadow. The sine of the Sun’s longitude is the sine of the arm of the true Sun including the precession of the equinoxes. By this truth, the "Parahita" <note>a system of astronomical calculation</note> readings are followed. To obtain the precise ascensional difference <note>term: "cara"; the difference between the tropical and equatorial rising times</note>, a few minutes of arc should be subtracted from the Sun’s sine. Because of the variation in the diurnal circle, there is a possibility of variation in the ascensional difference; therefore, those multipliers are shown: "The son is wealthy," "The sun is earth," etc., are the sines for the solar signs. By that strength, the sine of the Sun <note>literally "cool-rayed sun"</note> becomes clear in the calculation of the ascensional difference mentioned here. Here, the previously mentioned Gulika is accepted in the selection of an auspicious moment <term>Muhurta: a period of 48 minutes or the general science of electional astrology</term> according to those who know the tradition. Regarding the four blemishes, he says: "From the Sun’s constellation..." From the constellation where the Sun is located, at night, always, in the morning or evening, the tenth constellation...

| 6. T1: Meteor from the position | |

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astronomical omens and mathematical calculations for planetary positions, specifically focusing on the "five flaws" or malefic points in the sky.</meta>

<page-num>15</page-num>
<header>Chapter One</header>

# Chapter One

<sup>1</sup> A meteor <note>original: "ulka"</note> is neutralized at the rising of Venus, and a tremor <note>original: "kampa"</note> is neutralized when the Moon is sighted. The "Staff" <note>original: "danda"</note> and the "Banner" <note>original: "dhvaja"</note> cause no harm if the Ascendant is joined with or aspected by benefics. || 68 ||

On the eighth, ninth, seventh, and first lunar days <note>original: "tithis"</note>, <sup>2</sup> the Banner, Meteor, Tremor, and Staff are considered to be auspicious in that specific order. || 69 ||

From the degree of the sign where the Sun is positioned on that day, and also in the <sup>3</sup> directions and constellations <note>original: "ashadibhe"</note>, starting from that region, the <sup>4</sup> agitated <note>original: "dhuta"</note> time-periods <note>original: "ghatikas"</note> are measured, beginning with seven <note>original: "shaila," literally "mountain," a code-word for the number 7</note>. || 70 ||

Smoke <note>original: "dhuma"</note>, the Solar Anomaly <note>original: "vyatipata"</note>, the Halo <note>original: "parivesha"</note>, Indra’s Bow <note>the rainbow</note>, and the Comet <note>original: "ketu"</note>—these five are to be known in sequence according to the verses spoken hereafter. || 71 ||

<sup>5</sup> Add four signs and thirteen degrees <note>original: "vishvamsha," where Vishva represents the number 13</note> to the Sun's position; [this is Smoke]. Subtract this from the full circle <note>original: "vilome," in reverse</note> to find the Solar Anomaly. Add six signs to that to find the Halo. Subtract that from the full circle <sup>6</sup> to find Indra's Bow. Add seventeen degrees <note>original: "atyashti," the number 17</note> to that to find the Comet. These five stars <note>or points</note> should be avoided. || 72 ||

At night, the flaw called the Meteor occurs. The seventh constellation <note>original: "nakshatra"</note> from the Sun’s constellation is always the flaw named Tremor. Likewise, the fifteenth constellation in the forenoon is called the Staff of Brahma <note>original: "brahmadanda"</note>. The twenty-first constellation in the evening becomes the Banner. In these four, the time-periods measured by seven and so on are particularly agitated. On the eighth, ninth, and other <sup>7</sup> lunar days, <sup>8</sup> the Banner, Meteor, etc., are auspicious and not harmful. This is the meaning. The position of the Sun in a portion of a constellation—even in the tenth constellation and others—starting from that place, the aforementioned seven-fold time-periods are agitated.

He describes the five flaws beginning with Smoke: "With thirteen degrees..." When the Sun is combined with four signs and thirteen degrees, the resulting constellation is the flaw named Smoke. When that Smoke-Sun is reversed (subtracted from the circle of 12 signs), the observed constellation is the <sup>9</sup> Solar Anomaly <note>original: "vyatipata"</note> flaw. When <sup>10</sup> six signs are added to that Solar Anomaly, it becomes the Halo. When that is subtracted from the circle, it is named Indra’s Bow. When seventeen degrees are added to that, it is named the Comet. Thus, the five flaws are to be known. When one sign is added to that Comet, the true position of the Sun <note>original: "suryasphuta"</note> is also established.

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
<page-num>16</page-num>

->The Jewel of Auspicious Moments<-

When there is a favorable combination <term>Yoga: a mathematical relationship between the Sun and Moon</term> on a Sunday, or when the Moon is in a specific constellation <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 lunar mansions</term>, or when the Moon is <gloss>1</gloss> well-placed <gloss>2</gloss> strong, or when a benefic planet is in a cardinal house <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</term> or in its sign of exaltation: || 73 ||

The five defects, beginning with "Smoke" <note>original: "Dhumapurvakah" referring to the Upagrahas or secondary planets like Dhuma</note>, do not cause harm in these cases. <gloss>A</gloss> These <insert>are</insert> neutralized. Now, regarding the constellations derived from the combination of the Sun and Moon: the 2nd, 9th, <gloss>3</gloss> and the constellation itself || 74 ||

The 13th, 6th, and those named Rudra <gloss>4</gloss> and Dhriti <note>The 11th and 18th constellations</note>, and the 19th constellation—these constitute the Seven Defects which are destructive. || 75 ||

<gloss>5</gloss> Opposition, Iron Bar, Thunderbolt, Staff, Fragment, Spear, and the one known as the "Great Fall" <note>original: "Vyatipata"</note>—all of these are like poisonous snakes. || 76 ||

They are not broken by exceptions, nor by strong favorable qualities. [But] when Jupiter, possessing full rays and complete strength, is positioned in a cardinal house or a trine <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term> from the Moon, then even the most powerful defects are destroyed, just as the Great God <note>Shiva</note> destroyed the world-poison. || 77 ||

When the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is aspected by or occupied by Jupiter, Mercury, or the Lord of the Ascendant, and <gloss>6</gloss> not by others, <gloss>7</gloss> the defects of the combinations vanish, as do the Seven Defects. || 78 ||

<gloss>8</gloss> Hasta, Mula, Swati, Anuradha, Revati, Magha, <gloss>9</gloss> Ardra, and Ashlesha: when these constellations coincide with the Sun and Moon, the "Striking" defect and others occur in sequence. || 79 ||

Striking, Iron Bar, Thunderbolt, Great Fall, the Unfavorable, Knot, Great Knot, Spear, and including the Support—these are the nine. || 80 ||

---

Regarding the Seven Defects: "From the Sun and Moon..." "Possessing rays" means endowed with light. <gloss>10</gloss> "Lord" means the ruler of the zodiac sign.
Regarding the Nine Defects: "Hasta..." "Preta" refers to the Magha constellation. Among the nine defects like "Striking," the specific durations (ghatikas) are mentioned...

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing and unfavorable planetary combinations, specifically focusing on the calculation of the "Eka-argala" defect.</meta>

# Chapter One

-> Three, three, nine, thirty, fifteen, nine, and seven; <-
-> These are the specific durations <note>measured in Nadikas, a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> to be avoided at the end of the Vyaghata, Parigha, and other unfavorable yogas. || 81 || <-
-> If the latter half of the Anuradha constellation <note>original: "Maitra"</note> coincides with the union of the Sun and Moon, <-
-> Then the extremely censurable defect known as "Sarpa-mastaka" <note>literally "the Serpent's Head"</note> occurs. || 82 || <-
-> In the yogas of Vyaghata, Shula, Parigha, Vyatipata, <-
-> Ganda, Atiganda, and Kulisha, when all are present; <-
-> The constellations of Punarvasu, Rohini, Magha, Ashlesha, Ashvini, Mula, <-
-> Anuradha, Pushya, and Chitra are considered to be at the "Head." || 83 || <-

The final portions of these periods are especially to be avoided. If the latter half of Anuradha <note>original: "Maitra"</note> occurs during the nine defects starting with Vyaghata, a great defect called <term>Eka-argala</term> <note>a "single bolt" or obstruction that cancels out auspicious timings</note> arises in the upper portion. To identify this, the text lists the constellations located at the "Head" of the <term>Kharjarika Chakra</term> <note>a diagnostic astrological diagram shaped like a date-palm tree</note> when Vyaghata, Shula, and others are observed: Punarvasu <note>original: "Aditya"</note>, Rohini <note>original: "Chandra"</note>, and so on. The term "Sura-vardhaki" <note>the divine architect</note> refers to the constellation Chitra.

The constellations mentioned here are the "Head-constellations." If one asks how these are established by logic, it is said: the yoga-constellation <note>the point of union</note> that arises from the Sun and Moon being positioned between a pair of stars is situated at the "Head" of those two. 

**For example:** The Moon is in the middle of Krittika and the Sun is in the middle of Pushya. Their union results in the end of Magha. In this case, Krittika is the 23rd constellation from Pushya. Therefore, the 12th constellation from Pushya—and counting in reverse, the 12th from Krittika—is Mula, which becomes the "Head-constellation" at the Magha union. 

Alternatively, the phrase "between a pair of stars" refers to the interval between two constellations. For instance, if the Sun is at the end of the third quarter of Dhanishta <note>original: "Vasubha"</note> and the Moon is at the end of the first quarter of Swati, their union also occurs at the end of Magha. Here, Swati is the 9th from Dhanishta; thus, the 5th from Dhanishta—and counting in reverse, the 5th from Swati—is the "Head-constellation" at the Magha union. That too would be the root constellation. However, in the calculation starting from Dhanishta, because the numbers are equal, there is no "middle" as such. This logic should be applied everywhere.

<summary>This page details the calculation of the "Eka-argala" and "Sarpa-mastaka" astrological defects, explaining how to identify "Head-constellations" using the Kharjarika Chakra diagram. It provides mathematical examples for determining the point of union between the Sun and Moon relative to specific lunar mansions.</summary>

<keywords>Eka-argala, Kharjarika Chakra, Sarpa-mastaka, Vyaghata, Shula, lunar mansions, Indian astrology, planetary union, Anuradha, Magha</keywords>

<header>The Jewel of Auspicious Moments</header>

# Now, the Flaws of the Single Bolt and Others.

->Wise men state that one should draw one vertical line<sup>1</sup> and thirteen horizontal lines;<-
->this is the diagram known as the "Date Palm" <note>original: khārjjūrika</note> diagram.<-
->In the context of the Vyaghata <note>one of the twenty-seven yogas or astronomical divisions</note> and others, the "head star" is mentioned; there, when<-
->the Sun and Moon reside on the same<sup>3</sup> line, the wise call that visual alignment the "Single Bolt" <note>original: ekārgala</note>. || 84 ||<-
->Because this is favored in the Date Palm region<sup>4</sup>, it is called the "Date Palm"<sup>5</sup> diagram.<-
->Others, however, say it is because the lines resemble the leaves of a date palm. || 85 ||<-
->Counting forward and backward from Magha, Indra-Agni <note>Vishakha</note>, Ambu <note>Purvashadha</note>, and Paushna <note>Revati</note>, and from the tenth star from each;<-
->if the Sun and Moon's stars are equal in that count, it is the "Great Fall" <note>original: vyatipāta</note> flaw. || 86 ||<-

Regarding "one vertical line": One should draw one line going upward. This means it is extended toward the East. On that line, one should draw thirteen horizontal<sup>7</sup> lines, such that the first is at the base of the eastern line and the last is at the "throat" or top of the eastern line. In this way, the diagram containing twenty-seven line-ends is called the "Date Palm" <note>khārjjūrika</note> diagram. The star that is designated as the "head star" in the context of Vyaghata and other yogas is placed at the head of this diagram. This means one should place that star at the head of the diagram and then count the stars in order. In that diagram, when the Sun and Moon are situated on the same line, their visual alignment is the "Single Bolt" <note>ekārgala</note>. This implies that when the Sun and Moon are at the two ends of a single horizontal line, the Single Bolt flaw occurs. By the word "visual alignment" <note>dr̥kpāta</note>, the two ends are accepted. Since the vertical top does not have two ends, the Single Bolt would occur simply by the Sun and Moon occupying the ends of the lines. To establish this, it is said "situated on the same line." Some say this diagram is named "Date Palm" because it is favored—meaning accepted as a flaw—in the Date Palm country. Others say it is called "Date Palm-like." When the count of stars is performed forward and backward from the star Magha, its tenth star, and Mula, if the two stars occupied by the Sun and Moon are equal, then the "Great Fall" <note>vyatipāta</note> flaw occurs. Similarly, the "Great Fall" should be known by counting forward and backward from the Moon's star and its tenth star.

# Chapter One
Page 19

When the planet that causes a specific flaw is strong, <gloss>1</gloss>that flaw becomes fruitless (ineffective). When the Moon and Sun are in the divisions of benefic planets <gloss>2</gloss>and possess full strength, || 87 ||

and are simultaneously aspected by benefics, then the flaws arising from the conjunction of the Sun and Moon—such as *Vyaghata* <note>a specific astrological obstruction</note>, the "Serpent's Head" (*Sarpashirsha*), and the power of the "Single Bolt" (*Ekargala*) <gloss>3</gloss>—do not take effect. || 88 ||

The Minister of the King of the Gods <note>Jupiter/Brihaspati</note>, when positioned in the Ascendant (Lagna) with great strength and a shimmering web of rays, pacifies these flaws. A single Jupiter can overcome many difficult evils, just as a single prostration performed with devotion before the Wielder of the Trident <note>Lord Shiva</note> destroys many sins. || 89 ||

# The "Low" (Nimna) Muhurtas starting from Ashwini and others

*Dare, Sunu, Maya, Netre, Senamadhye, <gloss>4</gloss>Shuki, Payah, Haro, Giri, Tanu, Supa, <gloss>5</gloss>Shaka, Shoka, Patu, Narai.* || 90 ||

*Shukasunu, Shakasthana, Kakasthana, Sanimaya, Haro, <gloss>6</gloss>Jaro, Muni.* These are the "Low" (*Nimna*) <gloss>A</gloss> periods starting from the mansion Ashwini, which <gloss>7</gloss>one should avoid. || 91 ||

The words <gloss>8</gloss>*Dare*, *Sunu*, etc., are indicators of the *Muhurtas* <note>units of time, approx. 48 minutes</note>. In the word *Dare*, the two syllables represent two *Nadis* <note>units of 24 minutes</note>; in *Sunu*, two *Nadis* <gloss>9</gloss>... || 92 ||

<gloss>10</gloss>*Kesha, Naga, Sani, Krura, Ghara*—these apply to the *Nanda* <note>1st, 6th, 11th lunar days</note> and others in sequence. One should avoid these "Low" periods, as well as the two *Ghatis* <note>48 minutes</note> before and after them. || 93 ||

---

> Regarding "in the divisions of benefic planets": this should be connected with "aspected by benefics at that time." The *Muhurtas* indicated by the pairs of syllables in the text starting with *Dare* in the sequence of Ashwini etc., are called "Low" (*Nimna*). The explanation for this is found in the verse beginning "The words *Dare*, *Sunu*, etc." Regarding "*Kesha, Naga*": the two *Nadis* (minutes/units) indicated by the two syllables starting with *Kesha*—specifically the *Nadi* above and the *Nadi* below—are designated as "Low" <gloss>11</gloss>in the *Nanda* <gloss>12</gloss>and other lunar days. Regarding "of these": the specific details are given as *Kesha* and so on. Whatever exception exists for the afflicted Moon, that same <gloss>13</gloss>exception applies to these "Low" periods.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of Muhurta (electional astrology), specifically focusing on the lunar conditions that are considered inauspicious and the exceptions that mitigate them.</meta>

<page-num>20</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Electional Astrology <note>original: "Muhurtaratnam"</note></header>

## Now, regarding the Afflicted Moon and its Exceptions ##

->From the hair onwards, the indicators of the pulses <note>nadi</note> are shown with their divisions.<-
->Hair is in the first part; two pulses in the second; seven pulses in the riverbank.<-
->Two pulses are born in the place of residence; one should infer them elsewhere too.<-
->Located in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth house, joined with a malefic, or in a malefic division,<-
->The Moon of "evil action" is of six types and brings death in both fortnights.<-
->Thus the six types are stated; the resolution for the afflicted Moon,<-
->Spoken through the words of the Ascendant and its division <note>navamsha</note>, is also told here.<-
->Located in the center <note>kendra</note> of the Ascendant or its division, or in the center of a benefic's occupied division,<-
->In the twelfth-part division of a benefic, or in the center of the sign belonging to the lord of the time-hour <note>kala-hora</note>.<-

## Now, the Empty Stars in the months starting with Chaitra ##

->Aspected by the lord of its own division, the six-fold Moon becomes a bringer of growth and good deeds.<-
->Aries and Ashvini; Chitra and Swati; Vishakha and Pushya; Punarvasu;<-
->Dhanishta and Purva Phalguni; Uttarashadha and Purvashadha; Shatabhisha and Revati; Ajaikapada.<-

---

Therefore, he demonstrates that afflicted Moon and its exceptions. "Located in the sixth or eighth," etc. That Moon whose lunar activity <note>chandrakriya</note> is corrupted is called "evil action." The resolution for the Moon which brings death through the six types mentioned is spoken of in the verse and a half starting with "Ascendant and its division." That is explained here below. By the word "resolution," the exception <note>apavada</note> is meant. He states that exception: "Located in the center of the Ascendant or its division." The "twelfth-part" <note>dvyanshansha</note> refers to the *dvadasamsha*. The "sign of the division" is the zodiac sign of the lord of one's own state-division, and that belongs to the lord of the time-hour <note>kala-hora</note>. "By the lord of the division" means by a benefic who is the lord of the Moon's division. "Good deeds" means one who performs good actions. Here, the specific order is not intended <note>original: "vivakshita"</note>, as it is established by itself. He names the "Empty Stars" <note>shunyatara</note> in the months starting with Chaitra in order: In Chaitra, Aries and Ashvini; in Vaishakha, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, and Pushya; in Jyeshtha, the auspicious Punarvasu; in Ashadha, Dhanishta and Purva Phalguni; in Shravana, Uttarashadha and Purvashadha; in Bhadrapada <note>original: "Prosthapada"</note>, Shatabhisha and Revati; in Ashvin, Ajaikapada <note>Purva Bhadrapada</note>.

# Chapter One

Pushya, Indra <note>Jyeshtha</note>, Agni <note>Vishakha</note>, Magha, Mitra <note>Anuradha</note>, Shurpa <note>Vishakha</note>, Ahirbudhnya <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note> stars;
Hasta, Ashvini, Hara <note>Ardra</note>, <gloss>1</gloss> Mula, <gloss>2</gloss> Hari <note>Shravana</note>, Yama <note>Bharani</note>, and the Lord of the Asuras <note>Shatabhisha</note>. || 99 ||
In the months starting with Chaitra, one should avoid these "void stars" <term>shunya-tara: stars considered inauspicious for specific months</term> which are measured by their respective positions.

### Regarding the Names of the Lunar Months

The lunar months are known as Chaitra and Vaishakha,
Jyeshtha, Ashadha, Shravana, and Proshthapada <note>Bhadrapada</note>;
The one called Ashvina, Kartika, Margashirsha,
Pausha, Magha, and Phalguna; these end with the full moon or new moon. || 100 ||

### Regarding the Examination of the Months

When the pair of stars starting with Chitra <note>Chitra and Svati</note> are joined with the Full Moon,
the months starting with Chaitra are to be understood; the sixth, last, and seventh months are determined by groups of three stars. || 101 ||
<gloss>3</gloss> Five, five, twelve, one, four, <gloss>4</gloss> ten, one, eleven, etc., one, two, etc. <note>This refers to a numerical code for calculating void dates</note>;
<gloss>5</gloss> In the three-eighths division, the first and others in the dark fortnight, <gloss>6</gloss> others should be avoided. || 102 ||
Aquarius, Pisces, Taurus, the Lute <note>Lyra/Veena, likely referring to a specific stellar configuration</note>, Aries, <gloss>7</gloss> Virgo, the eighth is Libra;
Sagittarius, Cancer, Capricorn, and Leo are to be avoided in the months starting with Chaitra respectively. || 103 ||

In Kartika: Pushya, Indra, Agni, and Magha; in Margashirsha: Mitra, Shurpa, and Ahirbudhnya stars; in Pausha: Hasta, Ashvini, and Hara; in Maagha: Mula and Hari; in Phalguna: Yama and the Lord of the Asuras. "Measured by position" means mentioned in each respective place. "Ending in the festival" means concluding with the New Moon. Regarding "Starting with Chitra": when Chitra or Svati is joined with the Full Moon <gloss>8</gloss> it occurs, then it is considered the month of Chaitra. The same applies to the others. The sixth, last, and seventh months are to be determined by the three stars each that are joined with the Full Moon. This is a general rule, <gloss>9</gloss> not without exceptions. Among the fifteen months <note>original: "shararksha-adi," referring to specific counts</note>, in the bright fortnight, the fifteen lunar days <term>tithis</term> starting from the first are "void." In those same months, in the dark fortnight, the <gloss>10</gloss> second and following dates are called "void." There, in the penultimate month, the <gloss>11</gloss> fifteenth and the first. And so, in the *Barhaspatya* <note>Treatise of Brihaspati</note>: "<gloss>12</gloss> In the bright fortnight of Kartika, the fourteenth is not good, nor the fifteenth in the dark. The Full Moon and the New Moon in Margashirsha, and the first day are not good." In Chaitra and the following months, Aquarius and the other mentioned zodiac signs are "void."

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology
<note>original: "Muhurtaratnam"</note>

->Page 22<-

The pairs of Libra-Capricorn and Leo-Capricorn are to be avoided; likewise, Cancer-Sagittarius are said to be forbidden.
The pairs of Leo-Scorpio <note>original: "Simha-Ali"</note>, Jupiter-Mars <note>referring to their respective signs Pisces and Aries</note>, and Taurus-Pisces are forbidden on odd lunar days <term>Tithis: the 30 phases of the moon used in the Vedic calendar</term> in their respective order. || 104 ||

Sagittarius, Libra, Aries, Gemini, Leo, and Libra <note>The text repeats Libra/Juka</note> are givers of death.
These, along with the fourth sign from each, are forbidden on even lunar days starting from the second, in their respective order. || 105 ||

When joined with benefics <note>planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a strong Moon</note>, the "void" constellations, lunar days, and zodiac signs attain merit.
A benefic planet situated in a center <term>Kendra: the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</term> or a trine <term>Trikona: the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</term> destroys the defects of "void" stars and other such faults. || 106 ||

Those known as "month-void" stars or signs, or those called "poisonous,"
do not cause defects if they are aspected by or joined with their own lords, Jupiter, or Mercury. || 107 ||

The "state of being joined with benefics" for the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the sign rising on the eastern horizon</term> is proclaimed as "joining with the auspicious."
The constellation occupied by the Sun is "blind"; following that, the next three groups of three constellations each have specific qualities. || 108 ||

---

Sagittarius and the others, joined with their respective fourth signs, are to be known as "givers of death" on the even lunar days from the second until the twelfth. Accordingly, in the *Treatise of Brihaspati* <note>An ancient authority on law and astrology</note> it is said: "On the twelfth lunar day, Capricorn and Pisces—these are the 'death-giving' combinations." On the lunar days from the first until the thirteenth, the pairs of signs mentioned in the verse beginning with "Libra-Capricorn" are to be avoided. Thus, in the *Treatise of Brihaspati*: "On the thirteenth day, Taurus and Pisces—these houses are known as 'burnt' <term>Dagda: a technical term for inauspicious signs</term>." The explanation of the previously mentioned "Ascendant" follows: regarding the phrase "attains merit when joined with benefics," it refers to the Ascendant sign being in conjunction with auspicious planets. Previously, in the context of "blind" combinations, the "blind constellations" favored by some were mentioned. Now, the "blind constellations" are described in their proper order: "The constellation occupied by the Sun." The constellation where the Sun is positioned is "blind." The nine constellations following that, in groups of three, are sequentially considered "blind" <note>This refers to a system where certain stars lose their power or become "blind" based on the Sun's position, making them unsuitable for certain activities</note>.

---

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing for the Upanayana (sacred thread) ceremony, specifically focusing on neutralizing the "blindness" of certain stars.</meta>

# Chapter One

->**The Neutralization of Stellar Blindness**<-

Those known as blind, one-eyed, or possessing sight <note>original: "andhaka-kāṇa-dṛk-dṛkkāṇa"; these refer to specific classifications of zodiacal degrees or stars that are considered "blind" or "sighted" in electional astrology</note>. If a benefic planet is in an Upachaya house <note>The 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses, associated with growth</note> from the Ascendant, or if the Moon is in its sign of exaltation <gloss>1</gloss> or in a trine <note>The 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note>, it becomes a <gloss>2</gloss> bringer of auspiciousness. Such a Moon, increasing in strength, destroys the "blindness" of a birth star. || 109 ||

When a "two-eyed" star <note>A star considered to have full astrological sight</note> is rising at the Ascendant, and the Moon is in an <gloss>3</gloss> auspicious division, the Moon brings good fortune. Endowed with strength, it shall destroy the defect caused by the "blindness" of a star. || 110 ||

If a strong benefic planet is in a quadrant <note>The 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note> of the Ascendant, and that Ascendant is a "two-eyed" star, or if the Moon is full and auspicious, the blindness of the star is destroyed. || 111 ||

In the <gloss>4</gloss> sacred thread ceremony <term>Upanayana: the initiation ritual where a student receives the sacred thread</term>, if the Sun is <gloss>5</gloss> situated in the twelfth house and is aspected by Mars, it is said to cause blindness for the teacher. However, <gloss>6</gloss> that blindness does not occur if the Moon is auspiciously placed. || 112 ||

If the Lord of the Ascendant is placed in the 1st, 2nd, <gloss>7</gloss> 3rd, 12th, or 11th houses, and <gloss>8</gloss> Saturn and Mars are <gloss>9</gloss> both situated in "two-eyed" stars, the teacher shall not suffer blindness. || 113 ||

When the Lord of the 9th house <note>The house of dharma and religion</note> or the Lord of the Ascendant is placed in a <gloss>10</gloss> "two-eyed" star, the teacher will not go blind, just as one is protected by the grace of Lord Shiva. || 114 ||

The <gloss>11</gloss> first and last triads are called "blind." The second, fifth, and eighth triads are called "one-eyed." The third, fourth, sixth, and seventh triads are endowed with "two eyes" (full sight). Regarding the phrase "When a two-eyed star is rising at the Ascendant": this means when the star occupying the Ascendant possesses two eyes. In this section on exceptions to blind stars, the author demonstrates the exception to the blindness that might befall a teacher during the sacred thread ceremony: "In the ceremony..." etc. "Situated in the twelfth": the word "own" implies the Ascendant chosen for the time of the ceremony. If the Sun is in the twelfth house from the initiation Ascendant and is seen by Mars, the teacher becomes blind. There is also a reading: "But for the Sun in the last house." <gloss>12</gloss> Thus it is said in the *Madhaviya*: "Mars, fully beholding the Sun situated in the twelfth house, makes the teacher blind." And in the *Barhaspatya*: "If Mars beholds the Sun when it is in the twelfth house from the Ascendant."

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology
<page-num>24</page-num>

->The first quarters of the pairs starting with Hasta and Vasu, Indra and Agni, and Ahirbudhnya and Ahinatha, respectively, are known as the "Disease of the Day" (Dinamaya). || 115 ||<-

->The first quarters of the pairs starting with Sarpa and Budhnya, Mula and Yamya, <gloss>1A</gloss> Shravana and Ajeshya, and <gloss>2</gloss> Soma and Vayu, respectively, are known as the "Death of the Day" (Dinamrityu). || 116 ||<-

->These two afflictions are harmless at night, and even during the day if the Moon is strong. <gloss>3</gloss> Count from the Sun’s current constellation to the constellation Mula; that same number counted forward from Mula is called <gloss>4</gloss> Kandaka. || 117 ||<-

->The constellation Sthuna is found similarly from Mars. The sum of these two counts from Mula identifies the constellation Kandaka-sthuna. || 117 ||<-

->If the planets causing Kandaka-sthuna and the others are in <gloss>5</gloss> their own divisions or exaltation, or are aspected by benefics, they destroy the faults they would otherwise cause. || 118 ||<-

->Vandya, Chandra, Tanu, Divya, Naga, <gloss>6B</gloss> Nitya, and Ruru are the names for the specific periods. The "Half-Strike" (Ardha-prahara) <term>Ardha-prahara: A specific inauspicious period of time lasting about 1.5 hours</term> ghatikas <term>Ghatika: A unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> should be known starting from Sunday. || 119 ||<-

Regarding the verse "Hasta and Vasu": This means the first quarter of the first pair of constellations, the second quarter of the <gloss>7</gloss> second pair, and so on. These two are the faults named "Disease of the Day" and "Death of the Day." Regarding "Sun": <gloss>8</gloss> Whatever the number is from the constellation occupied by the Sun to Mula, the constellation that same number away from Mula is called Kandaka. <gloss>9</gloss> Whatever the number is from the constellation occupied by Mars to Mula, the constellation that same number away from Mula is called Sthuna. Furthermore, <gloss>10</gloss> by adding the number counted from the Sun to Mula and the number counted from Mars to Mula, the constellation at that combined distance from Mula is called Kandaka-sthuna.

Regarding the "Half-Strike" periods: "Vandya" refers to the 14th ghatika. "Chandra" refers to the 26th ghatika. In this manner, the ghatikas for all days are to be known. In the *Madhaviya* <note>A famous 13th-century astrological treatise by Madhavacharya</note>, the specific watches (yamas) associated with these ghatikas are shown: "The Half-Strikes on the days starting with Sunday are at 4, 7, 2, 5, 8, 3, and <gloss>11</gloss> 6 units." <gloss>12</gloss> Here, "Aruna" means the Sun. A "unit" (ansha) is an eighth <gloss>13</gloss> part of the day.

<summary>This page details the calculation of specific inauspicious astrological time-slots (Dinamaya, Dinamrityu, and Ardha-prahara) and dangerous constellations (Kandaka, Sthuna) based on the positions of the Sun and Mars.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing and planetary afflictions, specifically focusing on the calculation of Yamakantaka and the duration of planetary impurities in specific lunar mansions.</meta>

# Chapter One

The fault of the "half-strike" <note>original: "ardhaprahara-dosha"; a specific inauspicious time period</note> does not exist when the Ascendant and the Sun are endowed with strength. The divine, the venerable, and the policy-driven are known by the wise as the Moon and the mountains <note>original: "naga"; likely referring to the number seven or fixed points</note> in sequence. The periods of **Yamakantaka** <note>The "Restrainer's Thorn," a highly inauspicious sub-period of the day ruled by Jupiter's "son"</note> should be avoided, starting from Sunday onwards. **120**

The benevolent Yamakantaka, when situated in the Ascendant, a cardinal house, or a trine, or when the Moon is in an "Upachaya" <note>houses of growth: 3, 6, 10, and 11</note> house, results in good deeds when aspected by benefics. **121**

The three signs joined with or released by the Sun are desired. In all works, [afflicted signs] should be avoided; however, the rising of Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus is auspicious. **122**

One should abandon a lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra: one of the 27 sectors of the lunar path</term> occupied by a planet, as well as its adjacent mansions, and the mansion where an eclipse occurs. Likewise, those mansions where planets undergo retrograde motion, combustion <note>disappearing due to proximity to the Sun</note>, or planetary war <note>conjunction of two planets</note> should be avoided. **123**

A mansion joined by Mars and other planets returns to its natural state after three months, a moment, six months, three days, or a year respectively; or [alternatively] after twenty multiplied by twelve days. **124**

---

Thus, the time-periods <note>original: "nadika"</note> should be known even for the aforementioned Yamakantaka. In the phrase "a mansion joined by a planet and its adjacent ones," the word "mansion" refers to a Nakshatra. The "eclipse mansion" is that Nakshatra in which an eclipse occurs. The fault of an eclipse mansion arises only *after* the eclipse has occurred, not before, unlike the "Vaidhrita" <note>a specific astronomical yoga</note> and other points. Accordingly, in the **Brihaspatya** <note>a classical text attributed to the sage Brihaspati</note> it is said: "The fault occurs only after the eclipse, never before. Just as the faults of a house burning occur only after the fire has started." Those Nakshatras in which the retrograde motion of planets begins, or where the combustion of planets begins, or where planetary war occurs, are to be avoided. Likewise, in the **Madhaviya** <note>a famous astrological treatise by Madhavacharya</note> it is said: "The day in which planets set, or begin their retrograde motion, or in which festivals of the gods or various portents occur [should be avoided]." When a mansion is corrupted by planetary conjunction, it returns to its natural state after a period of three months and so on, once the planet has departed. "Multiplied by twelve" refers to the corruption caused by conjunction with the Sun.
