# || Now Begins the Second Chapter ||

## On the Favorability of the Menstrual Flow

<gloss>1</gloss> Starting from the seventh star, Ardra <note>The "Moist One," a lunar mansion associated with the star Betelgeuse</note>, and counting the next four <gloss>2</gloss> <note>original: "Veda-numbered," as there are four Vedas</note>, and then again
<gloss>3</gloss> four <gloss>4</gloss> <note>original: "the faces of the Creator (Brahma)"</note> <insert>[also]</insert> measured, and then the twelve <note>original: "Mitra-numbered," referring to the twelve Adityas or solar deities</note> in sequence.
<gloss>4</gloss> These stars, at the occurrence of the first <gloss>6</gloss> menses of women, make them respectively:
<gloss>5</gloss> addicted to pleasure, a wanderer <note>original: "kulata," a woman of loose conduct</note>, a widow, or endowed with many virtues. || 1 ||

This rule should be applied to an unmarried girl; now the rule for a married woman will be told.
In the groups of four and three, and likewise in the <gloss>7</gloss> ten directions <note>referring to specific sets of lunar mansions</note>, the auspicious and inauspicious results are considered in order. || 2 ||

<gloss>8</gloss> In the groups of thirteen, twelve, four, seven, and four stars, she is respectively: noble, enjoyable, clever, <gloss>9</gloss> firm, and common to both. Another method is now described. || 3 ||

---

Now, after knowing the strength and weakness of the defects, and since the time for Vedic rituals has arrived—and among those, the marriage ritual is primary, and that depends on the prior knowledge of the auspicious or inauspicious state of the girl—the author speaks of the auspicious and <gloss>10</gloss> inauspicious results based on the time of the first menses in the verse beginning "Starting from the seventh, Ardra." For women, at the time of the first menses, one should count seven stars starting from Ardra. Then again, four stars should be counted. Then again, four. Then twelve. These four types of stars make the <gloss>11</gloss> girl possess the characteristics mentioned. One who wanders among families is a "kulata" <note>a wanderer</note>; the meaning is "wicked." <gloss>12</gloss> The faces of the Creator are four. The Mitras are the Adityas <note>solar deities</note>.
!An unmarried girl is <gloss>13</gloss> "without teeth" <note>metaphor for being young/uninitiated</note>. The "method" is the rule. In the "stars of the directions"—Krittika, Magha, Anuradha, Dhanishta, etc.—if the first menses occurs in the first group of four, the girl is auspicious and good. In the following group of three, she is inauspicious. Alternatively, for a married woman, in the counts of thirteen, twelve, etc., the results like <gloss>14</gloss> "noble" and so on occur. Now the common method for both married and unmarried is declared. The meaning is that the result is the same for both.

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
## Page 52

-> On the contact with auspicious items, in the forenoon, at the rise of a favorable day, and under a favorable <term>aspect (Drishti)</term> <note>The angular relationship between planets</note>, the outcome is known by the wise to be auspicious; otherwise, it is inauspicious. (4) <-

In **Ashvini**, she quickly becomes a widow; in **Bharani**, she will have many daughters. In **Krittika**, she will be childless; in **Rohini**, she brings grief to her husband. (5)

In **Mrigashirsha** <note>original: "Saumyarkshe"</note>, she becomes of bad character; in **Ardra**, she associates with the low-born. In **Punarvasu** <note>original: "Adityam"</note>, she is prone to wandering; but in the **Pushya** mansion, she is blessed with sons. (6)

In **Ashlesha**, her children die; in **Magha**, she is full of pride. In **Purva-Phalguni**, she is of bad character; in **Uttara-Phalguni**, she is endowed with offspring. (7)

In **Hasta**, she is always wealthy; but in **Chitra**, she might abandon her husband. In **Svati**, there will be suffering from heart disease; in **Vishakha**, she becomes a widow. (8)

In **Anuradha** <note>original: "Maitre"</note>, she is devoted to cruel deeds; in **Jyeshtha**, she enjoys the company of her husband's younger brother <note>original: "Devaram"</note>. In **Mula**, she is always wealthy, but death may occur during childbirth. (9)

In **Ashadha** <note>Purva/Uttara</note>, she is devoted to the work of a servant, following elephants and horses. In **Abhijit** <note>original: "Vaishvarkshe"</note>, she is always fortunate; in **Shravana**, she will be barren. (10)

In **Dhanishtha**, she is sinful and devoted to many husbands. In **Shatabhisha** <note>original: "Varune"</note>, she will be of a sorrowful nature and possessed of some faults. (11)

In **Purva-Bhadrapada**, she is wealthy with good sons, of animal-like <note>instinctive or stubborn</note> nature, and high-minded. In the second Bhadrapada (**Uttara-Bhadrapada**), she finds happiness; in **Revati**, she is wealthy and beloved. (12)

When the Moon is joined with Saturn, the Sun, Mars, or Rahu <note>original: "Phanibhih" - the hooded ones</note>, it does not grant desired results. Even if the omen is inauspicious, consider it auspicious if joined by a benefic planet; then she shall not face death. (13)

***

**Commentary:** Regarding "Auspicious": *Devara* means the husband's brother. *Hasti-ghota* refers to the retinue of elephants. Regarding "Even if inauspicious": If the Moon is joined by a benefic planet, even if the result of the lunar mansion is inauspicious, it becomes auspicious. "She shall not face death" refers to this protection.

***

# Chapter Two

If the first menstruation occurs under the birth star <note>original: "janmarkṣe"; the lunar mansion occupied by the Moon at the time of birth</note>, there will be suffering from disease. If it occurs during the "Destruction" star <note>original: "naidhanam"; the 7th star from the birth star in the sequence of 27</note>, it bestows death. If it occurs when the Moon is in the eighth sign from the husband's sign, the woman <note>original: "vāśitā"; a term for a woman or a female elephant</note> meets with death. || 14 ||

In this way, one should understand the good and bad fortune when a maiden becomes a woman <note>original: "ṛtumatī"; reaching the age of menstruation</note>. If it occurs in the division of Mars, she becomes a servant to others; in the division of Venus, she suffers poverty. || 15 ||

In the division of Mercury, she enjoys sons and wealth; in the division of the Moon, she is of bad conduct. In the division of the Sun, she is rich in intelligence; in the division of Jupiter, she is illustrious. || 16 ||

In the division of Saturn, the maiden who becomes a woman is of meager fortune. If it occurs on the first day of the lunar fortnight, she cooks her own food <note>implies a lack of servants or a humble life</note>; on the second, she has few enjoyments. || 17 ||

On the third day, she is completely satisfied; on the fourth, she is devoid of wealth. On the fifth, she possesses full power and riches; on the sixth, she is devoid of good character. || 18 ||

On the seventh day, she is endowed with majesty; on the eighth, she becomes like a demoness. On the ninth, there is great gain; on the tenth, she is distinguished. || 19 ||

On the thirteenth day, she is radiant <insert>or "full of splendor"</insert>; on the fourteenth <note>original: "bhūtāyām"</note>, she becomes a woman of low conduct. On the full moon day, there is great gain; on the new moon day, she becomes the lowest. || 20 ||

If the rising sign is Aries, she will be poor; in Taurus, she will follow the "way of the cow" <note>original: "godharmā"; interpreted in the commentary as acting according to her own will or whims</note>. In Gemini, she shows an interest in enjoyments and wealth; in Cancer, she ruins both families <note>her father's and her husband's</note>. || 21 ||

In Leo, she will give birth only once; in Virgo, she will have many daughters. In Libra, she is like a balance-holder <note>fair or business-minded</note> and gives birth to many sons. || 22 ||

In Scorpio, she follows others <note>interpreted as unfaithful</note>, is of bad conduct, and is proud. In Sagittarius, she is devoted to her husband; in Capricorn, she is sweet-spoken. || 23 ||

---

->**Commentary**<-

"Destruction" <note>naidhanam</note> refers to the eighth star from the birth star. The word *Vāśitā* is a synonym for woman. Regarding the phrase "becomes a woman": if she menstruates for the first time under her birth star, there will be suffering from disease. At that time, the "Destruction" star bestows death. This is how the good and bad results should be applied. When a maiden becomes a woman in the divisions <note>sub-sections of a zodiac sign</note> of Mars and the others, the results occur as stated. *Bhūtā* refers to the fourteenth day of the lunar fortnight. "Way of the cow" <note>godharmā</note> means acting according to one's own pleasure or will.

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
<page-num>54</page-num>
->**The Jewel of Auspicious Moments**<- <note>original: "Muhūrtaratna"</note>

In Aquarius, there is love for both families and excellent religious fortune.
In Pisces <gloss>1</gloss> <note>variant: "poverty"</note> she will be afflicted by poverty and a girl becomes a woman <note>original: "ṛtumatī," referring to the onset of menstruation</note>. || 24 ||

<gloss>A</gloss> <note>The meter appears broken here.</note> Mental anguish, devotion to the husband, sorrow, obtaining a son, <gloss>2</gloss> all kinds of wealth,
Good fortune, and loss of wealth—these are the results for those who <gloss>3</gloss> flower <note>original: "puṣpitā," a poetic term for menstruation</note> on Sundays and the following days of the week. || 25 ||

[Thus ends] the section on the suitability of the onset of menses. Now begins the section on suitability for marriage:
For women, a man born in the "Danger," "Obstacle," or "Death" stars <note>The 3rd, 5th, and 7th stars (Nakshatras) from the birth star are traditionally considered inauspicious</note> from their three birth-cycles should be avoided.
In these cases, those born in the <gloss>4</gloss> first, fourth, and third quarters <note>original: "ku-veda-agni-aṃśa," using numerical code words: Ku (Earth/1), Veda (4), Agni (Fire/3)</note> are especially to be avoided in order. || 26 ||

From the second birth-cycle, those born in these <gloss>5</gloss> first, fourth, and third quarters are to be avoided.
From the third birth-cycle, all those born in <gloss>6</gloss> fierce quarters are abandoned. || 27 ||

The repetition of the <gloss>7</gloss> first, fourth, and third quarter rule is not desired here.
Revati, Ashvini, Anuradha, Shravana, <gloss>8</gloss> Jupiter <note>Pushya</note>, Rohini, Jyeshtha, and Ardra are the Gods.
The <gloss>9</gloss> Humans are Bharani, the three Purvas, and the three Uttaras with Rohini <note>The text lists specific stars belonging to the Human category</note>.
The rest are Demons. The results [of unions between these groups] are quarrel, death, fear, and prosperity.
In God-Demon and Demon-Human unions there is conflict; in Human-God unions, it is equal. || 28 ||

---

"Flowered" <note>puṣpitā</note> means "having reached menstruation." Now, the auspicious and inauspicious results arising from the relationship between the birth stars of the woman and the man are discussed.
"Born etc.": From the three birth-cycles of women, a man born in the third, fifth, or seventh stars—known as "Danger" <note>Vipat</note>, "Obstacle" <note>Pratyari</note>, and "Death" <note>Naidhana</note>—is to be avoided. Even among these stars, those born in the <gloss>10</gloss> first, fourth, and third quarters are especially avoided. Thus, the excess of fault for those born in these quarters during the first birth-cycle should be known. In the second birth-cycle, these men born in the first, fourth, and third quarters are avoided. In the third birth-cycle, those born in "fierce" quarters are avoided. If <gloss>11</gloss> two people are born in the same star, both are avoided. Here, in the third cycle, the repetition of the specific quarter rule is not intended; only the "fierce" nature of the quarter is considered. The second birth star is technically called the "Action" <note>Karma</note> star. The third birth star is called the "Conception" <note>Ādhāna</note> star. Even in calculating the auspicious moment <note>Muhurta</note>, the fault of these "Danger" stars etc. is considered. In the joining of God, Demon, and Human groups <note>Ganas</note>, quarrels and so on occur in order. This is "Group Compatibility" <note>Gaṇānukūlya</note>. What was mentioned previously was "Day Compatibility" <note>Dinānukūlya</note>.

---

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of matrimonial compatibility (Kuta) based on the lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and zodiac signs (Rashis).</meta>

# Chapter Two

->The Mahendra agreement, the ocean, the mountain, and the directions <note>original: "abdhy-adri-dik"; these are numerical codes (Sankhya) representing 4, 7, and 10</note> are auspicious when counted from the bride's birth star. A groom's birth star that is very distant from the bride's birth star is also considered to bring auspiciousness. (29)<-

->The Horse, Elephant, Goat, Sheep, Serpent, Mongoose, Cat, Rat, Cow, Buffalo, Tiger, Deer, Monkey, and Dog; these are declared the "Yonis" <note>term: Yoni (source/origin); in astrology, this refers to the animal temperament associated with a birth star</note> of the stars. Natural compatibility among these is auspicious. (30)<-

->A groom born in the second, third, fourth, fifth, or ninth zodiac sign from the bride's birth sign should be avoided. Others are acceptable, as is a groom from the same sign but a different lunar mansion. (31)<-

->For women born under odd-numbered signs <note>term: Oja-rashi (Odd signs like Aries, Gemini, etc.)</note>, the sixth and eighth signs from their own are considered mediocre. For those born under even-numbered signs <note>term: Yugma-rashi (Even signs like Taurus, Cancer, etc.)</note>, the sixth is censurable but the eighth is auspicious. (32)<-

->Unity or friendship between the planetary lords of the groom's and bride's birth stars is highly recommended.<-

***

**Commentary:**
Regarding "Mahendra"—this refers to the Mahendra agreement. Here, the birth of the man is intended to be in the specified stars. Regarding "from the bride's birth"—this refers to the "Samjna" <note>naming/classification</note> or being very distant, such as fifteen stars away. 

Regarding "Horse"—the term "Goat-wife" <note>original: "ajavadhu"</note> is a single compound. "Cat" <note>original: "akhu-bhuj", literally 'rat-eater'</note> refers to the domestic cat. "Musha" is a female mouse, and "Akhu" is a male mouse. "Lulaya" is a buffalo. "Sarangika" is a female deer. "Kauleya" is a dog <note>literally: 'of a kennel'</note>. "Plavaga" is a monkey. 

"Natural compatibility" refers to the inherent friendliness between the animal types of the bride's and groom's birth stars; such a bond is auspicious. Regarding "from the bride's birth star"—this refers to the "Rashi" <note>zodiac sign</note> agreement. "Born in odd signs" means for women born when the Moon is situated in an odd-numbered sign. "Recommended" refers to the agreement known as "Adhipati" <note>Lordship/Planetary friendship</note>.

***

<margin>The Mahendra agreement is counted as the 4th, 7th, or 10th star from the bride's.</margin>

<margin>The animal types (Yonis) determine the physical and temperamental compatibility of the couple.</margin>

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
<page-num>56</page-num>

->**The Muhurtaratnam**<-

Leo and Scorpio are the seventh and fourth <note>original: "muni-krta" (7 and 4)</note>; Sagittarius and eighth <note>Scorpio</note> are for Aries;
Libra is the third and Pisces the tenth; Capricorn is the sixth and Aquarius the fourth.
<gloss>1</gloss> Pisces and Aquarius; <gloss>2</gloss> Aries and Scorpio; the first and Capricorn—these
are the signs subject to the "Vashya" <term>Vashya: Mutual attraction or control between zodiac signs</term> of Aries and the others, as stated by me in order. || 33 ||

<gloss>3</gloss> From Ashvini <note>original: "Dasra"</note>, groups of three should be placed <gloss>4</gloss> on three fingers in clockwise and counter-clockwise order.
If the birth stars of the couple fall on the same finger-joint, they must be avoided. || 34 ||
If they fall on the middle finger, it causes death or enmity.
<gloss>5</gloss> Ashlesha, Revati, and Jyeshtha <note>original: "Sarpa, Paushna, Indra"</note> along with their third and ninth stars; their fifth and seventh;
<gloss>6</gloss> their sixth; and their fourth and eighth; these are the <gloss>7</gloss> forbidden "Vedha" <term>Vedha: Obstruction or piercing; a negative astrological alignment</term> stars. || 35 ||

From Ashvini, the elements <note>original: "Bhuta"</note> Earth and the rest are assigned in counts of five, six, five, six, and five.
The union of the same element is auspicious; the union of Fire and Water <gloss>8</gloss> is forbidden; others are neutral. || 36 ||

The stars of the Universal Gods, Indra, the Final, Water, the Self, the Lord of Wealth, <gloss>9</gloss> Chitra, Fire, the Serpent, the Four, and Yama are
Female or Neuter; Mula, Shatabhisha, and the Moon-star <note>Rohini</note> are also such; the others are Male. || 37 ||

---

"Leo and Scorpio" refers to the category called **Vashya**. "Muni-krta" means the seventh and fourth. Thus, in the *Barhaspatya* text:
"For Aries, the Vashya signs are Leo and Scorpio; for Taurus, they are Cancer and Libra." Some say if the man's birth sign is the Vashya-sign of the woman's birth sign, it is "Vashya" (subservient/compatible). <gloss>10</gloss> Others say if <gloss>11</gloss> either birth sign falls in the Vashya-sign of the other, it is compatible.

"From Ashvini" refers to the **Rajju** <term>Rajju: Literally 'rope'; a method of matching based on the parts of the body</term> calculation.
Starting from Ashvini, <gloss>12</gloss> and from Rohini, groups of three are counted in reverse order, and so on.

"Ashlesha etc." refers to the **Vedha** calculation.
The stars Ashlesha, Revati, and Jyeshtha form one Vedha group. Then, the third and ninth stars from those three form another group of six. Then, the fifth and seventh from those same stars form another group of six. Then, the sixth stars from them form a group of three. Then, the fourth and eighth <note>original: "naga" (8)</note> from them form a group of six.
If the birth stars of the woman and man fall within the same group, it is forbidden. Compatibility exists where there is no Vedha.

"From Ashvini, Earth etc." <gloss>13</gloss> refers to the **Bhuta** (Elements) calculation.
"Others are neutral" means they are of middling quality.

"Universal Gods etc." refers to the **Yoni** <term>Yoni: Biological/instinctive compatibility, often categorized by animal types or gender</term> classification.
In this context, a birth in one's own Yoni category is considered to bestow auspicious results.

---

# Chapter Two

The Brahmin, Kshatriya, Vaishya, and Shudra <note>The four traditional social classes of ancient India</note>, as well as those born of mixed unions in direct and inverse order;
<insert>1. From these stars</insert> Starting from Ashvini <note>original: "Dasra," the first lunar mansion</note>, the stars are to be assigned in order. Unity of caste is remembered as bringing auspicious results. || 38 ||

<insert>2. Bharunda</insert> The Bharunda <note>A mythical two-headed bird</note>, the Pingali <note>an owl</note>, the Crow, the Rooster, and the Peacock;
<insert>3. From Ashvini</insert> Starting from Ashvini, these are assigned in groups of five, six, five, and five <note>original: "shara," meaning five</note>. Unity of the bird-class is excellent. || 39 ||

The Mothers <note>Divine female energies</note> are those born of Brahma, the Six-faced One <note>Kartikeya</note>, Varaha, Hari <note>Vishnu</note>, and the Discus-bearer.
They are the energies of Kali, Ishvari, and Shri; born of Brahma, Guha, the Boar, and Vishnu. || 40 ||

->The Seven Sages <note>Saptarishi</note> are to be assigned starting from Ashvini; this distinction of Sages brings prosperity. || 41 ||<-

<insert>5. (A)</insert> From Ashvini to the birth stars of the woman and the man, the sum of those numbers <insert>6. including Abhijit</insert> is taken.
Subtracting thirty-two <note>original: "danta," meaning teeth/thirty-two</note> and dividing by five <note>original: "shara"</note>, if the <insert>7. remainder</insert> is an odd number, it is excellent. || 42 ||

<margin>If the woman is feminine and the man is masculine, it is auspicious. If the man is masculine and the woman is feminine, it is moderate. If the two are reversed, it is painful. Having the same gender is not desired.</margin>

The term "Brahmin etc." refers to the classification of castes. The stars starting from Ashvini, those from Hasta, and those from Mula—along with the triad starting from Purva Bhadrapada <note>original: "Ajaikapada"</note>—represent the Brahmins, Kshatriyas, and Vaishyas. As stated in the *Barhaspatya* <note>A text attributed to the sage Brihaspati</note>:
> "The three stars starting from Purva Bhadrapada belong to the three twice-born classes."

The reason for mentioning the inverse order after the direct order here is to suggest that when there is unity of caste, no difference or conflict is perceived. The term "Bharunda" refers to the classification of birds. "Mothers" refers to the category of maternal deities. "Seven Sages" refers to the classification by Rishis. Here, the stars starting from Ashvini, including the intercalary star Abhijit, are to be counted in four groups of seven. As stated in the *Barhaspatya*:
> "Among the stars starting with Ashvini, the wise should include Abhijit. Using four circles, the <insert>11. union of sages</insert> is applied here through calculation."

Regarding the calculation for the couple: count from Ashvini to the woman's birth star and the man's birth star separately. Add these two numbers together, add thirteen to the sum, subtract <insert>12. thirty-two</insert> from that, and divide the remainder by five. If the remainder is an odd number, it is <note>A. The original root text says "excellent," but the commentary notes it as "inferior" (adhama) in some readings.</note>. If thirty-two cannot be subtracted, he will later describe the calculation in reverse order.

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of matrimonial compatibility (Kuta) from the previous section, focusing on the hierarchy of importance among the twenty factors and specific animal-based temperament matches.</meta>

<page-num>58</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Electional Astrology</header>

->**The Main Compatibility Factors**<-

Thus, among the twenty types of compatibility described for a man and a woman, the following are considered the most important: **Zodiac Sign** <note>Rashi</note>, **Lord of the Sign** <note>Rashisha</note>, **Subservience** <note>Vashya</note>, **The Great Indra Factor** <note>Mahendra</note>, and **Sexual Compatibility** <note>Yoni</note>. || 43 ||

Regarding the verse "Gods and Demons," consider this specific distinction regarding the **Temperament Groups** <note>Gana</note>: If the man belongs to the Divine or Demonic group and the woman belongs to the Human group, || 44 ||

The results are, respectively, excellent and middling; the reverse is considered inauspicious. The Elephant and Horse, the Cow and Demon <note>Rakshasa</note>, the Goat and the **Cat** <insert>originally written as 'Bilala' in the manuscript</insert> || 45 ||

These pairs provide mutual benefit to one another. However, the Tiger and the Serpent bring death. The **Cat** and the Serpent, and the **Rat** and the Serpent, are mutual enemies. || 46 ||

The Buffalo and the Horse are mutual enemies. The Lion, however, provides auspiciousness everywhere. The Dog, Rat, Cat, Tiger, and Serpent are all mutual enemies to one another. || 47 ||

The wise know that the fourth star from the man's birth star is the **Great Indra** <note>Mahendra</note> factor, and the one following that is the **Lesser Indra** <note>Upendra</note> factor. || 48 ||

---

**Commentary:**
Regarding "Thus": Among the twenty types of compatibility described for a man and a woman, the Sign, the Lord of the Sign, and others are the most powerful. Here, three additional compatibilities will be mentioned: Income/Expenditure <note>Aya-Vyaya</note>, the Pandya method, and Caste <note>Jati</note>. Including these, the total is twenty. Therefore, the statement "twenty-fold" is flawless. Compatibility of age is not determined solely by the birth star. From the man's birth star, this is specifically the Mahendra factor. Others are specific variations already mentioned.

Now, he explains a specific detail not previously mentioned regarding the "Gods and Demons" verse: They are excellent or middling. When the man is of the **Divine group** <note>Deva-gana</note> and the woman is of the **Human group** <note>Manusha-gana</note>, the resulting marriage union is **excellent**. When the man is of the **Demonic group** <note>Asura-gana</note> and the woman is of the **Human group**, the resulting marriage union is **middling**. The reverse is inauspicious. If the woman is of the Divine or Demonic group and the man is of the Human group, it is respectively inauspicious or very inauspicious.

He mentions a specialty regarding the animal types <note>Yoni/Mriga</note> mentioned earlier: "Elephant and Horse," etc. "Rishya" refers to a deer. "Bastaka" refers to a goat. "**Bidala**" refers to a cat. Because of the abundance of animal types, this is called "Animal Compatibility" <note>Mriga-anukulya</note>. Regarding "From the birth star," this refers to the specific Mahendra calculation.

|---|---|---|---|
| 1. | T: "Principal" (variant reading) | 7. | T1, TA: "Signs and others" (variant reading) |
| 2. | T: "Quality/Temperament" | 8. | T1: "Middling" |
| 3. | T: "Bilalaka" (Cat) | 9. | From "When" until "middling" in the second line, there is a **manuscript lacuna** <note>missing text</note> in the T1 manuscript. |

---

# Chapter Two

->In the *Mahendra* <note>A specific method of calculating compatibility</note> agreement, one gains wealth and grain; in the *Upendra* agreement, one is blessed with progeny.<-
->Capricorn-Leo, Aries-Virgo, Libra-Pisces, and Cancer-Aquarius;<-
->Taurus-Sagittarius, and Scorpio-Gemini; these are said to be very auspicious <note>original: "su-shubham"</note> 6th-8th positions. 49.<-

->In the 6th-8th positions, there are the faults of death, enmity, and separation;<-
->In the 2nd-12th positions, there is poverty and lack of children; in the 5th-9th <note>trine</note> positions, likewise.<-
->In the remaining positions, there are various kinds of happiness, children, and wealth;<-
->Auspiciousness occurs immediately if the lords of the signs are friendly. 50.<-

->Even in the 6th-8th and other positions, if there is "Enmity-Piercing" <note>term: Vaira-vedha; a specific astrological conflict</note>,<-
->this fault does not apply if there is unity or "Subservience" <note>term: Vashya; a calculation of attraction</note> between the signs.<-
->In the case of "Hostile Yonis" <note>term: Viruddha-yoni; biological incompatibility between the birth stars</note>, even if there is a favorable trine relationship,<-
->it is certain to cause the loss of children in the "childless signs." 51.<-

Regarding the verse beginning with "Capricorn-Leo": the pairs of signs mentioned are called the 6th-8th sign combinations, and they are said to be very auspicious. Right here—the meaning is "highly favorable." Regarding "In the 6th-8th": in the remaining positions not mentioned <insert>as auspicious</insert>. Even when there is an unfavorable sign combination like the 6th-8th, or when there is "Enmity-Piercing" <gloss>Vedha</gloss>—meaning when the lords of the signs are enemies or a "piercing" conflict occurs—auspiciousness occurs immediately (meaning quickly). If there is friendship between the lords of the signs <gloss>Rashipa</gloss>, or if there is unity or "Subservience" <gloss>Vashya</gloss>, then this fault of the signs does not exist. "Trine relationship" <gloss>Bhakona-bhava</gloss> means the 5th-9th relationship between the two signs. That <insert>in certain cases</insert> can be a "destroyer of offspring." The "childless signs" are Virgo, Scorpio, Taurus, and Leo. <note>original: "Kanya-Ali-Go-Harayah"</note>

***

1. Reading from Manuscript T₁; Manuscripts T and M read "and with Aquarius" (Ghatena cha).
2. Manuscript M reads "Taurus-Archer-Scorpio."
3. Manuscript M reads "In the 6th and 8th houses, for the sake of great auspiciousness."
4. Manuscript T₁ reads "loneliness"; Manuscript M reads "due to lack of wealth."
5. Reading from Manuscript T₁; Manuscripts T and M read "going/moving."
6. Manuscript T₁ has a spelling error (Sheshashva).
7. Manuscript M reads "wealth for the sake of happiness."
8. Reading from Manuscript M; Manuscripts T and T₁ read "arising from" (bhava).
9. Manuscript T reads "Vairadhe" (Enmity-fixed); see the root text.
10. Manuscript T reads "Rashichayo-mitra" (collection of signs).
11. Manuscript T reads "Pashye" (in seeing).
12. Manuscript T reads "Bhakone" (in the trine).
13. Manuscript T reads "Stikonah" (the trine).

A. <margin>Note: In both the root text and the commentary here, it says "very auspicious" (su-shubham). However, in common worldly practice, the 6th-8th position is accepted only as "inauspicious" (ashubham). Perhaps the text here should read "the 6th-8th is inauspicious."</margin>

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta), specifically focusing on "Vedha" (obstructions or piercings) between stars and zodiac signs during marriage rituals.</meta>

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology
<note>original title: Muhūrtaratnam</note>

-> The mutual piercing of the "half-stars" in marriage: Ardra and Shravana; <-
-> Ashvini and Jyeshtha; Magha and Revati; and others in regular and reverse order. || 52 || <-

-> For Aries and Scorpio, Leo, Aquarius, and Sagittarius are enemies. <-
-> For Libra and Taurus, Capricorn and Cancer are always enemies. || 53 || <-

-> For Virgo and Gemini, Leo, Aries, and Taurus are enemies. <-
-> For Cancer: Aries, Leo, Libra, Capricorn, Aquarius, and Sagittarius— <-
-> and Taurus are declared enemies. For Leo: Pisces and Scorpio, <-
-> Taurus, Libra, Gemini, Virgo, Aquarius, and Cancer are enemies. || 55 || <-

-> Aries, Scorpio, and Leo are enemies for Sagittarius and Pisces. <-
-> Cancer, Scorpio, and Leo are enemies for Capricorn and Aquarius. || 56 || <-

-> Piercing at the head kills the husband; piercing at the throat kills the wife. <-
-> Piercing at the navel kills the offspring; piercing at the thighs causes the destruction of the lineage. || 57 || <-

---

Regarding the unfavorable combinations for marriage <note>technical term: Vivāha</note>, he speaks of the "half-stars" <note>Ardhabhāni</note>. The half-stars—Dhanishtha, Mrigashira, and Chitra—pierce each other. Ardra and Shravana pierce each other. Likewise, Ashvini and Jyeshtha. Likewise, Magha and Revati. Other pairs of stars not explicitly mentioned that pierce each other are to be calculated in regular and reverse order from the pairs already stated (Ardra-Shravana, Ashvini-Jyeshtha, and Magha-Revati). For example: the star above Ardra is Punarvasu; the star before Shravana is Uttarashadha. These two pierce each other. Likewise, the star above Punarvasu is Pushya, and the one before Uttarashadha is Purvashadha; these two pierce each other. Likewise, Ashlesha and Mula. Likewise, Purva Phalguni and Uttara Bhadrapada. <insert>Aryaman (Uttara Phalguni) and Purva Bhadrapada.</insert> Also Hasta and Shatabhisha. The section beginning "For Aries and Scorpio" describes the state of enmity <term>Shatrubhāva: a condition where certain zodiac signs are naturally discordant</term>. Regarding the aforementioned "piercings," he describes the specific results: "Piercing at the head," etc.

---

<page-num>61</page-num>
<header>Chapter Two</header>

->**The Effects of Vedha (Interference) and Age**<-

Piercing of the feet <note>original: "pada-vedha"</note> brings about great confusion and poverty. In the half-constellations, there is piercing of the head; in the half above that, it resides in the throat. || 58 ||

In the constellations near those, other piercings are also considered in sequence. The sequence of stars from the birth star are: Birth, Prosperity, Adversity, Prosperity, Obstacle, Achievement, Fatality, || 59 ||

Friendship, and Great Friendship; these cycles of birth stars and others repeat again and again. A man whose age is three times that of the woman brings auspiciousness. || 60 ||

Twice the age is said to be mediocre, and anything slightly more than that is inferior. If the woman is older than the man, there is no doubt it leads to the extinction of the lineage. || 61 ||

Longevity and daily happiness result in delightful life-satisfaction. Similarly, a favorable "Gana" <note>A classification of stars into Divine, Human, or Demonic temperaments</note> union is pleasing, and the "Mahendra" connection grants sons and grandsons. || 62 ||

The woman's star should be known as auspicious; a favorable "Yoni" <note>An astrological classification based on animal types to determine physical compatibility</note> brings wealth. Being of the same zodiac sign but different constellations increases sons and grandsons. || 63 ||

If the birth stars are identical, there is a fear of poison; if they are in the seventh sign from each other, there is great mutual affection. If the stars fall on the junction of signs or in a 2-12 relationship <note>where one sign is immediately behind the other</note>, it causes extreme poverty. || 64 ||

Rohini, Ardra, Shravishtha, Pushya, Mula, and Magha—these six: if the birth star of the couple is the same single star among these, it gives great sorrow. || 65 ||

Ashadha, Bharani, Hasta, Ashlesha, Jyeshtha, and Shatabhisha—these six: if the birth star of the couple is the same, it gives loss of life and separation from wealth. || 66 ||

---

The author explains the divisions of the head, throat, etc., established by the *Vedha Chakra* <note>The "Interference Wheel," a diagram used to check if planets or stars "pierce" or afflict one another</note> with the phrase "In the half-constellations." In the half-constellations, there is piercing of the head. Again, in the half above those, there is piercing of the throat. Again, in those situated near them, there is piercing of the navel. Again, in those situated near those, there is piercing of the feet. <insert>A</insert> <note>The commentator notes a missing line here regarding the thighs</note>. The phrase "Three times the woman's age" refers to the age combination. He states the results for some of the mentioned factors with "Longevity." From the union of a "Fatality" star <note>Vadha Nakshatra</note>, there is a loss of life due to the "day-fault" <note>Dina-dosha</note>. From the "Mahendra" quality, the attainment of sons is established; this is proven by the demonstration of results here. Regarding "Identity of stars," this refers to general results. "Rohini, Ardra..." and "Ashadha, Bharani..." refer to specific results.

# The Jewel of Electional Astrology

->The mutual attraction between the stars Magha and Shravana,<-
->and between Bharani and Jyeshtha, or Magha and Shravana;<-
->likewise between Hasta and Ashvini, or Krittika and Ashadha:<-
->these marriage unions grant happiness and sons. || 67 || <note>Magha is associated with the Ancestors (Pitri) and Shravana with Vishnu (Vaishnava). Bharani is associated with Yama and Jyeshtha with Indra.</note>

->The union beginning with Purva Phalguni and ending with Shatabhisha increases the lineage.<-
->But the combination of Dhanishtha and Pushya destroys the lineage, without a doubt. || 68 || <note>Purva Phalguni is associated with Bhaga; Shatabhisha with Varuna.</note>

->If the Moon or the Ascendant <term>Lagna: the rising sign on the eastern horizon</term> of the couple<-
->falls in Virgo, Scorpio, Taurus, or Leo, or in those specific divisions,<-
->then the achievement of having a son is very difficult to attain. || 69 ||

->In a constellation that spans two signs, the first part is blamed for the woman but praised for the man.<-
->The calculation starting from thirty-two <note>Sanskrit: danta, literally 'teeth', a numerical symbol for 32</note> in reverse order is the opinion of the Chola people. || 70 || <note>The Cholas were a prominent dynasty in Southern India; this indicates a regional astrological tradition.</note>

->Increase of sons, death, prosperity, disease, and wealth are the results.<-
->One should join the birth stars of the couple starting from Ashvini and multiply by five. || 71 ||

->Increase of wealth, calamity, and great fortune: this is the opinion of the Pandyas.<-
->Count from the birth star of the woman to the end of the stars and multiply by five. || 72 || <note>The Pandyas were another major South Indian dynasty with their own distinct scholarly traditions.</note>

***

Regarding "mutual attraction": this means the union of the two mentioned constellations <term>Nakshatras: the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac</term> brings auspicious results. The connection applies to Bharani with Jyeshtha, and the marriage union of Krittika and Ashadha. In the phrase "beginning with Bhaga," the first part is specified. Likewise, "ending with Varuna" uses the word "end" to refer to the star Revati <note>associated with Pushan</note>. "In a constellation that spans two signs" refers to a star that falls across two different zodiac signs. Regarding "from thirty-two": one should calculate the distance from the birth stars of the woman and man, subtract that value from thirty-two, multiply the remainder by five, and determine the result. This is the Chola method. The results like "increase of sons" etc., follow. "One should join from Ashvini" refers to the Pandya tradition; this rule is "the opinion of the Pandyas." "Counting from the birth star of the woman" refers to the calculation of income and expenditure...

***

# Chapter Two

->Divide the sum by seven <note>original: "munibhir" (by the seven sages)</note>; the remainder indicates the "Income" or "Expenditure" based on the birth star.<-
->Deer and Fish; Birds; Scorpion and Crab are the Reptiles.<- <note>These are classifications of the zodiac signs or stars into animal categories for compatibility.</note>

->Animals are the Deer, Lion, Goat, and Bull; the others are mostly Human.<-
->Union between the same species is best; between Animal and Human is middling.<-

->The union of an Animal with a Reptile or Bird is inferior.<-
->A union between an Animal and a Human <note>specifically "Tiryag" (horizontal/beast) and "Manushya" (human)</note> should never be performed.<-

->Rohini, Krittika, Moon (Mrigashira), Magha, Purva Phalguni, Uttara Phalguni,<-
->Anuradha, Jyeshtha, Mula, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, and Purva Bhadrapada—<-

->These are known as the "Increase" <note>Vriddhi</note> stars. Revati, Punarvasu, Ashwini, Bharani,<-
->Pushya, Chitra, Swati, Vishakha, Uttara Ashadha, and Shravana—<-

->These are known as "Debt" and "Expenditure" stars. Purva Ashadha, Ardra, and Hasta,<-
->along with Uttara Bhadrapada, are known as the "Great Prosperity" stars.<-

->In the "Great Prosperity" stars, there is always happiness; in the "Increase" stars, it is considered moderate.<-
->In the eight "Expenditure" stars, the result is known to be debt and loss.<-

Counting from the male's birth star to the female's birth star, multiplying by five and dividing by seven, the remainder is the "Income." If the remainder is seven, it is "Full Income." The terms "Deer," "Fish," etc., refer to the species classification. Compatibility is determined by the unity of species and the unity of the birth stars. The author explains the different names and results starting with "Rohini" <note>original: "Prajapatya"</note>. Some say this applies to the union of two consecutive stars in order. The reading is taken as "In the Expenditure and Increase stars" or alternatively "In the Expenditure and Debt stars."

<meta>This page continues the technical discussion of astrological compatibility for marriage, specifically focusing on the gender classification of lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and the transition into divinatory questions regarding the wedding ceremony.</meta>

<page-num>64</page-num>
<header>The Jewel of Electional Astrology</header>

->The five beginning with Jyeshtha <note>Jyeshtha, Mula, Purva Ashadha, Uttara Ashadha, and Shravana</note>, the three beginning with Dhanishtha <note>Dhanishtha, Shatabhisha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, the single foot of the next <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Ashvini, and the pair of Swati <note>Swati and Vishakha</note> are declared as masculine by the experts; the others are feminine. If both are masculine, it destroys suffering; if they are dissimilar, it is painful; but if both are the "Yamyam" <note>original: "yamya"; referring to the Bharani constellation or specific feminine pairings</note> feminine type, it is the most painful of all. If both are of the same feminine sign, wealth remains with them for a long time. || 80 ||<-

->Many masters of the tradition have taught this arrangement of genders. In the Moon’s Eight-fold Chart <term>Ashtakavarga: a system of points used to determine the strength of planets</term>, a higher number of white points <note>benefic points or 'bindus'</note> indicates the mental disposition of the woman and the man. || 81 ||<-

->Having understood their mutual attraction, one should then indicate their compatibility.<-

## Thus ends the section on Marriage Compatibility. Now, the Inquiry regarding Marriage.

->If malefic planets are situated in their signs of debilitation or in the houses of enemies, or occupy the Ascendant, the seventh, or the eighth house at the time of a marriage inquiry, it will not be auspicious for the couple. || 82 ||<-

->If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house from the Ascendant, and Mars is in the sixth or eighth from the Moon, the man will go to the God of Death <note>original: "Yama"</note> childless within eight years. || 83 ||<-

->The Moon, joined with a malefic planet and situated in the Ascendant, or in the sixth or eighth house, shall grant death to them within eight years of the time of marriage. || 84 ||<-

***

*The five beginning with Jyeshtha*—this refers to specific types of "Yoni" <term>Yoni: literally 'source', here referring to the biological/instinctive compatibility category of a star</term>. *Dissimilar* means conflicting. *Painful* means extremely difficult. If they are *similar*—meaning if both belong to the same birth-star category—wealth remains with them for a long time. *A higher number of white points in the Moon’s Eight-fold Chart* refers to having many points in the signs within one's own Moon-chart. If the other person's birth star falls there, it is praised. Likewise, the *mutual attraction* of the woman and man is praised. The meaning is that these two factors are especially auspicious. This concludes Marriage Compatibility. Now, the author describes the good and bad omens based on an inquiry <term>Prashna: the branch of astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</term>: *Malefics in debilitation*, etc. In some readings, the text says "when the Moon is joined with a malefic." In others, "the Moon in an Ascendant joined by a malefic."

***

# Chapter Two

If a malefic planet <note>original: "papa"; refers to planets like Mars, Saturn, or the Sun</note> is situated in an enemy's sign or its sign of debilitation, or is aspected by an enemy, and if this occurs in a malefic house, the maiden becomes a widow; otherwise, in a favorable house, she does not. **85**

When a malefic planet is in the fifth house, aspected by an enemy and in its sign of debilitation, that maiden will either be of loose character <note>original: "kulata"</note> or her children will die. **86**

When the Moon and Mars are joined in the first and seventh houses <note>the Ascendant and the house of marriage</note> at the time of a query <gloss>1</gloss>, her husband meets his end <note>original: "yamam yati"; literally "goes to Yama, the god of death"</note> in the seventh month. **87**

If the Moon and the Ascendant are in auspicious subdivisions <note>original: "shubhamsha"; specific 1/9th divisions of a sign</note> and are "with life" <note>possessing strength</note>, the astrologer should predict life for the couple. If those two have reached malefic subdivisions, there is no life for either of them. **88**

If the seventh house from the Ascendant is empty, <gloss>2</gloss> weak, and aspected by malefic planets, and is <gloss>3</gloss> devoid of the aspect of a benefic planet, the husband will be a <gloss>4</gloss> contemptible man <term>Kapurusha: a man lacking in courage, virtue, or physical capability</term>. **89**

<gloss>A</gloss> The Ascendant or the woman's birth Ascendant...
If <gloss>5</gloss> Mercury and Saturn are <gloss>6</gloss> in a movable sign in the seventh house, the husband will be a eunuch and a wanderer. If the Sun is in the seventh house and aspected by Saturn, the bride will be abandoned by her husband. **90**

---

**Commentary:**
Regarding the phrase "malefic in an enemy's or debilitated house": here, a malefic planet in the eighth house is meant. Because the word "eighth" from the previous verse carries over here, the eighth house is established. Alternatively, in the phrase "malefic <gloss>7</gloss> house," the word "malefic" means "destruction." Therefore, it means the "house of destruction" <note>the 8th house</note>. Or, there is a reading: "if this is in the house of longevity." So it is said in the *Barhaspatya* <note>an ancient text attributed to the sage Brihaspati</note>: "If a malefic is in the eighth house from the Ascendant, [aspected by an enemy or in debilitation, the maiden becomes a widow] if the Ascendant is joined with a malefic." *Kulata* means "wicked." Regarding "Moon and <gloss>8</gloss> Mars," there is also a reading "in the Ascendant and seventh respectively." <gloss>9</gloss> "With life" means the couple will be long-lived. "Kapurusha" means "vile." Here, <gloss>10</gloss> "the Ascendant or the woman's birth Ascendant": the previously mentioned combinations should also be examined from the woman's birth Ascendant. Those mentioned <gloss>11</gloss> hereafter should be examined specifically from the woman's birth Ascendant, according to those who know the tradition.

---

->**The Effects of Planets in the Seventh House**<-

If the Sun is aspected <note>aspected: in astrology, this refers to the "glance" or mathematical influence one planet casts upon another</note> by Mars or Saturn, the young woman will become a widow. If Saturn is in the descendant <note>the seventh house, governing marriage</note> and is aspected by enemies or malefic planets, she will become a widow while still a maiden. || 91 ||

If Mars and the Sun are in the seventh house, she becomes a widow in the seventh month or seventh year of marriage. <insert>Similarly,</insert> if Saturn and Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> are in the seventh house, she will become a "remarried woman" <term>Punarbhu: a woman who marries a second time, often viewed with specific legal/religious status in ancient texts</term> if that house is occupied by both auspicious and inauspicious planets. || 92 ||

If a malefic planet is weak and setting, yet aspected by a benefic planet, the woman will be abandoned by her husband. However, if the planet is in a debilitated or inimical sign, she becomes a <insert>prostitute</insert> <note>original: "Kaishiki," likely a scribal error for "Vaishiki"</note>. || 93 ||

If Venus and Mars are in each other's divisional signs, the bride will be attached to a lover. When the Moon and Venus are in the ascendant <note>the first house</note> while it is in a sign ruled by Saturn or Mars: || 94 ||

That woman becomes barren, unless a benefic planet occupies the house of children <note>the fifth house</note>. If Mars is in the sign or division of the descendant, or if the descendant is in a division ruled by Mars: || 95 ||

Or if Saturn is in the division of the descendant, the woman will suffer from diseases of the reproductive organs and be ill-fated. If malefic planets are in the fifth house, she will be barren or her children will die. || 96 ||

***

**Commentary:**
Regarding the verse beginning with "Arka": Some read the text as "If the descendant is aspected by the Sun, Mars, or Saturn, the young woman becomes a widow." Others read "Sun, Mars, and Rahu." In that case, they interpret the word "Yuvati" <note>literally 'young woman'</note> as referring to Rahu. Their own tradition is the authority for that. "Saturn and Rahu" <note>original: "Arkyahyo"</note> refers to the combination of Saturn and the Dragon's Head. "Remarried" means one who has wed twice. "The division of the descendant sign" refers to the specific portion of the seventh house. If Mars is there, or if the descendant falls in a division of Mars (Aries or Scorpio), or if Saturn is in the rising division of the descendant sign, she may lose her children due to causes such as miscarriage.

***

<margin>A. The reading from manuscript 'M' was chosen here to satisfy the requirements of both meter and meaning.</margin>
<margin>B. This should likely read "Vaishiki" <note>referring to a woman of the streets</note>.</margin>

# Chapter Two

> If the Moon is situated in the houses of debilitated planets or enemies, she herself becomes the killer of the son. This occurs if the Sun does not aspect <note>In astrology, "aspect" refers to a planet 'looking at' or influencing another house or planet from its position.</note> the Ascendant or the Arudha <note>The Arudha is a calculated point in a horoscope representing the manifestation of a house.</note> while in a debilitated or inimical sign. If the Sun is so placed, the woman has no father; if the Moon is so placed, she has no mother. || 97 ||

> When the Sun is in the seventh house from the Ascendant or the Arudha, or is in an inimical or debilitated sign and does not aspect them, the man’s father is not present. If the Moon is in such a condition, the mother is not present. || 98 ||

> If the Moon, Mars, Saturn, and Rahu <note>original: "sarpa" (serpent), referring to the north node of the moon</note> are in their own debilitated or inimical signs and aspect the girl, one should know her to be motherless, as has been stated. || 99 ||

> When the Moon aspects the Ascendant, the mother of the couple is happy. If the Moon does not aspect it, she is not present. The same should be judged for the father from the Sun and the seventh house. || 100 ||

> If malefic planets aspect while the Sun is in a debilitated or inimical sign, the father is either dead or will depart due to a conflict with the son. || 101 ||

> When the Moon is in an even sign during the dark fortnight <note>original: "Krishnapaksha," the waning phase of the moon</note> from the time of the query, and is aspected by malefics or is in the eighth house, the marriage alliance will not be obtained. || 102 ||

> If the Moon and Venus are in the Ascendant, or if the Ascendant is aspected by the Moon and Venus, the querent will obtain the girl without any opposition. || 103 ||

> If Venus and the Moon are strong, placed in even signs and in feminine Navamshas <note>A Navamsha is a one-ninth division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned analysis</note>, and if they aspect the Ascendant from "two-footed" <note>original: "dvipat," referring to human signs like Gemini or Virgo</note> signs, then the girl will be obtained. || 104 ||

> If the portion of the seventh house is associated with or aspected by benefics, the girl will be beloved by her husband. She will be prosperous and endowed with good qualities; of this, there is no doubt. || 105 ||

---

Regarding the verse beginning with "The Moon, Mars...": Here the word is "Moon." It should be understood that the Ascendant is aspected by the Moon and Venus. "Standing in two-footed signs" means being placed in masculine signs.

# The Jewel of Auspicious Moments
<page-num>68</page-num>
->The Jewel of Auspicious Moments<-

If Venus is in its own sign, its exaltation <term>Exaltation: The zodiac sign where a planet is at its maximum strength</term>, or its trine, <gloss>A¹: Variant reading: "Having good sons"</gloss> the bride will have virtuous sons and a long life. This is also true if Venus is in the rising sign of the Moon or in its own exalted sign. || 106 ||

If Jupiter is in the rising sign and the Sun is in its exaltation, or if Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus are in the seventh house from the rising sign or the <term>Arudha: The sign calculated based on the position of the house lord</term>, then || 107 ||

At the time of a marriage inquiry, one should declare: "The marriage will take place."
Touching the right arm, <gloss>³</gloss> the head, or the chest is auspicious for marriage.
However, touching the back of the left hand or the knee <gloss>⁴</gloss> is inauspicious for it. || 108 ||

<gloss>⁵</gloss> One should say the bride resides in the direction associated with the lord of the inquirer's Arudha sign.
Or, one should determine this by the direction of the lord of the rising sign <gloss>⁶</gloss> based on their relative strength. || 109 ||

If the lord of the seventh house looks upon <gloss>⁷</gloss> the seventh house from the rising sign, then the man is present near the astrologer, especially if aspected by Jupiter. || 110 ||

Whatever the complexion or appearance of the woman <gloss>⁸</gloss> seen or mentioned at that moment, it should be said that the marriage will be with such a woman. || 111 ||
<gloss>⁹</gloss> Thus ends the inquiry regarding marriage. Now, the auspicious timing for marriage.

Benefic planets <term>Benefics: Naturally favorable planets like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury</term> located in houses other than the 8th, 3rd, or 6th from the inquiry Arudha, the Moon, or the rising sign <gloss>¹⁰</gloss> are auspicious.
Malefic planets <term>Malefics: Naturally harsh planets like Mars or Saturn</term> in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses || 111 || grant auspiciousness in marriage. || 112 ||

---

Regarding "In its own sign, etc.": If a benefic planet is in the descendant portion and in its own sign, the bride will be endowed with virtuous sons and so on.
<gloss>¹²</gloss> He explains the results based on the body parts touched by the inquirer: "The right arm, etc." Thus ends the marriage inquiries. Now he explains the auspicious timing for marriage: "Inquiry Arudha, etc." This means that at the time of the chosen moment, the planets are expected to be in auspicious positions relative to the inquiry Arudha, the inquiry Moon, and the inquiry rising sign, as known from previous calculations. This is because the time of the inquiry grants results just like the time of birth.

A. Here the reading in Manuscript M is also correct.

# Chapter Two

If Jupiter is positioned in the 9th, 5th, 2nd, or 7th house from the girl’s birth ascendant <note>Lagna</note>, and the malefic planets are in the 3rd, 11th, or 6th houses, one should perform the marriage before the eighth year. <insert>113</insert>
After the twelfth year, Jupiter bestows auspiciousness in all positions. The measurement of time, such as the year, may be calculated by the Solar <note>Saura</note> or Civil <note>Savana</note> systems. <insert>114</insert>
For marriage, initiation <note>Upanayana</note>, tonsure <note>Kshaura</note>, and the offering of funeral cakes <note>Sapindikarana</note>, the measures of the day are determined by the Solar, Jovian <note>Jupiter-based</note>, Civil, Lunar, and Sidereal systems. <insert>115</insert>
The Sun is considered steady and favorable when situated in the latter half of Pisces, Sagittarius, or Cancer. However, the Sun in Virgo or Aquarius, if not aspected by Jupiter, is condemned. <insert>116</insert>
In the months of Isha <note>Ashvina</note> and Tapasya <note>Phalguna</note>, the Sun is not exceedingly harmful if aspected by Jupiter. The months starting from Chaitra are known as Madhu and Madhava;
->thereafter, the months are Shukra and Shuchi in order;<-
->Nabhas and Nabhasya are said to be Isha and Urja;<-
->Sahas, Sahasya, and then Tapas and Tapasya.<- <insert>117</insert>
When the constellations are counted in direct and reverse order and situated in the corresponding directions, and when the Moon and Mars are equal in strength, it is auspicious for marriage. <insert>118</insert>

**Commentary:**
"One should perform the marriage" <note>original: upayacchet</note> means one should conduct the wedding ceremony. Before the eighth year, the auspicious positions of the planets must be specifically investigated. For women after the age of twelve, Jupiter is a giver of auspiciousness regardless of its position relative to birth. "Measures of the day" <note>original: ahah-pramanani</note> refers to the specific daily measurements of that particular year. Regarding the term "Varanga," three or four days should be added at the completion of a cycle. "Kodanda" means the sign of Sagittarius. "The Sun not aspected by Jupiter" refers to the Sun in Virgo or Aquarius. "In Isha" refers to the month named Ashvina. "In Tapasya" refers to the month named Phalguna. The months of Chaitra, Vaishakha, etc., are given the technical names Madhu, Madhava, and so on. "Situated in the corresponding directions" refers to the group of constellations being located in the East, West, South, or North.

# The Gem of Auspicious Moments
<page-num>70</page-num>

->**The Gem of Auspicious Moments**<-

The three stars of Vishnu <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, Shatabhisha</note>, Punarvasu, Ashwini, Shatabhisha, and Chitra are not auspicious. However, Magha, Libra, the house of Mercury <note>Gemini and Virgo</note>, Sagittarius, and the latter half of Capricorn are excellent. || 119 ||

The "Empty" days <note>the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note>, the new/full moon days, the eighth lunar day, Vishakha, Purva Phalguni, Jyeshtha, Magha, and Bharani; Krittika and Ardra are associated with "Disease" <note>term: *Ruk* - a technical category of unfavorable times</note>. The sixth and twelfth lunar days are associated with "Grief" <note>term: *Shuk*</note>. || 120 ||

Malefic days <note>Tuesday, Saturday, Sunday</note>, Venus when set <note>invisible due to proximity to the Sun</note>, and benefics in the 6th, 8th, or 12th houses are unfavorable. Malefics are favorable in the 3rd, 6th, and 11th houses. These are endowed with the two designations of "Grief" and "Disease." || 121 ||

When the Moon is joined with Mars and the other planets in sequence, the results are: death, childlessness, misfortune, having a co-wife, renunciation <note>becoming a wandering ascetic</note>, and loss of wealth. || 122 ||

If the Moon is in the same zodiac sign as these planets, the mentioned faults are considered to be at half-strength. If the Moon is in the same lunar mansion <note>Nakshatra</note>, one should conclude that the faults are at their full intensity. || 123 ||

The Moon is auspicious when placed in the 3rd, 6th, 10th, or 11th houses from its natal position, or in the natal sign itself. Other authorities mention the new/full moon, the 8th, 14th, and 4th lunar days as significant. || 124 ||

Regarding these, the full moon day in the bright fortnight and the 8th day in the dark fortnight are intended by those who speak of "Obstacle-Actions" <note>activities likely to face interference</note> in both fortnights. || 125 ||

The group named "Disease." Even though it is designated as "Disease," Magha is excellent. The "Empty" days, the new/full moon, the 8th, and Vishakha etc., are designated as "Disease." They also carry the designation of "Grief." The 6th and 12th days carry the designation of "Grief." Malefic days and Venus when set also carry the designations of "Grief" and "Disease." "Excluding the 3rd, 6th, and 11th" means those not in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses. Loss of wealth occurs when joined with the Sun. As Brihaspati <note>the divine preceptor and authority on law and astrology</note> says: "With the Sun, the Moon makes one poor; with Mars, it brings death." There is no fault if they are in different signs <margin>It might also be "one star is censured"</margin>. "Mentioned by others" means they are remembered as auspicious <note>in specific contexts</note>. The explanation is found in the phrase "Regarding these." The expert in "Obstacle-Actions" does not accept the 4th and 14th days here, as they are considered equal by the knowers of tradition.

<meta>This page continues the discussion of astrological timing (Muhurta) for marriage, specifically focusing on planetary placements that predict the lifespan or dominance of the husband or wife.</meta>

<page-num>71</page-num>
<header>Chapter Two</header>

# Chapter Two

If a planet is in a different division <note>original: "amshaka"</note> in male signs, or in a male division in other signs, it is praised. The Moon situated in the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note> brings death; it is mediocre in the 5th, 9th, or 4th houses. || 126 ||
<gloss>1</gloss>Saturn and the Sun bring death; other planets are auspicious when shaken off. The Moon situated in the 5th, 9th, or 4th houses causes an abundance of daughters. || 127 ||
Mars <note>original: "asrik"</note> brings death or <gloss>A</gloss> praise; Rahu and Mars situated in the 8th house <note>the house of death</note>. If in the 8th from the Electional Ascendant <term>Muhurta Lagna: the specific auspicious moment chosen for a ceremony</term>, the Sun <gloss>2</gloss> is weakened if in a fall or enemy sign. || 128 ||
All planets in masculine signs are auspicious for the man; otherwise, for the woman. From the birth of the couple, if the Sun and Venus are in the 6th or 7th signs or divisions, they are <gloss>3</gloss> destroyed. || 129 ||
<gloss>4</gloss>If the lords of the 7th house and the Ascendant do not aspect <note>look at</note> their own houses, they cause the early death of the woman and man respectively. <gloss>5</gloss>By the lack of aspect from the lord of the 7th house or its division <gloss>6</gloss>, the death of the bride occurs. || 130 ||
By the lack of aspect from the lord of the Ascendant or its division <gloss>7</gloss>, the death of the <gloss>8</gloss> groom occurs. Lovers will be of the respective castes of the planets; if planets are in the centers <note>quadrants</note>, there were three previous ones. || 131 ||

<gloss>9</gloss> "Different division" means a division designated as feminine. "In others" means in signs designated as feminine. Regarding "Mars brings death or praise": the word "givers" is implied. "Dina" means the Sun. Regarding "planets in masculine signs": all planets in masculine signs are auspicious for the man. This means the man will be <gloss>10</gloss> beloved by the woman. <gloss>11</gloss> Those in feminine signs are auspicious for the woman. This means she will be beloved by the man. Alternatively, male and female planets are intended respectively. Thus says Brihaspati <note>a legendary sage and preceptor of the gods</note>:
> "In marriage, all male planets are strong when occupying male signs. The man shall be beloved <gloss>12</gloss> of women; in the opposite case, it is otherwise."

And Narada <note>a divine sage and messenger</note>:
> "In a time when male or female planets are strong, the strength of the man or woman follows accordingly."

"Destroyed" means troubled. The explanation of "un-aspected by the lord" is: "from the 7th house." By the lack of aspect from the lord of the 7th house and the division rising in the 7th house, first the <gloss>13</gloss> death of the bride occurs. By the lack of aspect from the lord of the Ascendant and the division rising in the Ascendant, the first death is of the <gloss>14</gloss> [groom]. Thus says Brihaspati:
> "If the Ascendant and its division at the time of marriage are not aspected by their lord, the man dies first; likewise, if the 7th house is so, the woman dies."

The form "mamāra" <note>he died</note> is used here as an archaic/sage usage.

| 3. T1: beneficial (variant reading) | 10. T: beloved of the woman |

A. In the root text "praise" (stuteḥ) is seen, but in the commentary "offspring" (suteḥ) appears. There is difficulty in determining the correct reading here.
